## I.2 What is being decided by this brief, and what is not

| This brief **does** | This brief **does not** |
|---|---|
| **Convert 22 formulated CEA validation questions and the corpus's own unresolved register into a single professional work package that counsel can receive and work from** | **Answer any of them** |
| **Separate the questions that can be answered before an entity exists from the questions that cannot** | **Select, prefer, rank or recommend an entity** |
| **State, for every question, the discipline, the minimum evidence, the dependency and the consequence of leaving it unresolved** | **Invent evidence, assume a fact, or upgrade an assertion to a finding** |
| **Preserve every blocker exactly where the corpus left it** | **Move a gate, amend a register, close a conflict or lift a prohibition** |
| **Surface the conflicts between the current architecture and the candidate legal forms** — principally **`GC-01`** | **Resolve them. *GC-01 is a human decision, not an analytical one*** |
| **Provide a copy-and-send instruction pack (Part XXIII) and a founder disclosure checklist (Part XXIV)** | **Perform the instruction. **Only a person can**** |

## I.3 The finding that governs how this brief should be used

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — **[S-K(E): U-PD-16, §XXVI.1]**, reproduced because it is a warning about this exact kind of document.

> ### **`U-PD-16: SIX INSTRUMENTS HAVE NOW PERFORMED `SR-01`'S STATED NEXT ACTION AND `SR-01` HAS NEVER ADVANCED, BECAUSE PRODUCING A PAPER IS NOT INSTRUCTING A PROFESSIONAL. A SEVENTH PAPER WOULD BE THE SEVENTH THAT DOES NOT ADVANCE IT.`**
>
> **This brief is a paper. On its own it advances nothing.**
>
> **It differs from the six before it in exactly one respect, and the difference is not analytical: it is written to be **sent**. Part XXIII is a standalone instruction pack that can be copied into an engagement letter without reference to any other document. Part XXIV is the disclosure checklist the founder completes before sending it. Part XXV fixes the format in which the answers must be returned, so that a reply can be filed as governance evidence rather than read and forgotten.**
>
> **If Part XXIII is not sent to a qualified Indian professional, this brief is the seventh paper that did not advance `SR-01`, and §XXX.2's qualification becomes the verdict's operative clause.**

## I.4 The three questions that need no entity, and why they are first

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **ID-1**, **PVR-03**, **PVR-05**, **PVR-22**; the dependency analysis at **CEA §XXIX.4**.

| # | **Question** | **Why it does not wait for `SR-01`** | **Part** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **PVR-03** | **Is partial payment of earned compensation lawful?** | It concerns the **treatment of people**, not the form of the body that treats them. Every answer is available on a stated set of facts about the relationship, the work and the payment — and the CEA's entire shortfall rule (**§XI.5 SS-0 … SS-6**) is gated on it | **VI** |
| **PVR-05** | **Is revenue recognised on collection or on invoice?** | It concerns **when a fact is recorded**, and the four different meanings of "revenue" can be separated without an entity. What an entity *changes* is which framework applies — and that dependency is stated, not assumed | **VII** |
| **PVR-22** | **Do the four unpaid control roles carry personal liability for the people holding them?** | It concerns **exposure that already exists**, in the present, for four named people performing functions today. **Unpaid status is not a liability position, and this is the only question in the register whose answer protects a person rather than a structure** | **VIII** |

> ### **`NINETEEN OF TWENTY-TWO CEA VALIDATION ITEMS AND SIXTEEN OF EIGHTEEN DEPENDENCY STEPS SIT BEHIND `ID-1`. THESE THREE SIT IN FRONT OF IT.`**

## I.5 The executive determination

| Determination | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **1** | **The architecture is not blocked by analysis. It is blocked by the absence of a legal person, an authority and a set of professional opinions.** Every one of the three is obtainable by an act of a person, and none is obtainable by an act of drafting |
| **2** | **`SR-01` / `LDR-U08` remains rank 1 and the root of the dependency tree.** **[S-F:604]** places it above `LDR-U01`, `A-N16`, `A-N10` and `LDR-U06`; `SR-02`, `SR-03`, `SR-04`, `SR-05`, `SR-12`, `SR-13`, `SR-14`, `SR-19`, `SR-20` and `SR-98` all carry `SR-01` as their dependency |
| **3** | **The evidence required for `SR-01` is *"a written decision **plus** counsel opinion"* — and the opinion comes before the decision** **[S-F:1878]**. An entity decision taken without the opinion does not close `SR-01`; it creates a new, undocumented risk |
| **4** | **No entity may be selected by this brief, by counsel's convenience, or by default.** The chain is `OPTION SET → CONSEQUENCE ANALYSIS → PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION → HUMAN DECISION`. The option set is **OPT-A … OPT-J** and it is **open** *(Rule G-17)* — a schedule for counsel to complete, not a menu to choose from |
| **5** | **The sharpest structural conflict is `GC-01`, and it is a human decision, not an analytical one.** The eight-priority waterfall is `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` and a reserved matter; the not-for-profit forms carry *"restrictions on profit distribution and founder upside"* and *"may not fit commercial waterfall without separate structure"*. **Priorities 4 and 8 may be incompatible with OPT-A, OPT-B and OPT-C. Whether they are preserved, amended or referred is `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` + `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION` (Tier-1) + `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** |
| **6** | **Nothing may proceed that spends, pays, collects, engages, contracts, insures or publishes.** Part XXVII lists the stop conditions in full and preserves every prohibition in the corpus's own words |
| **7** | **Three workstreams can proceed in parallel at no cost and no risk** — Part XXVI: the paper records capability, the founder disclosure pack, and the internal authority design that does not depend on the entity's form |

# PART II — MANDATE AND SCOPE

## II.1 The mandate

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — the CEA's **ID-1**, which is itself `[SOURCE]` from **[S-K(E): §XXVI.1]** and **[S-F:175, 604, 1878]**.

| Element | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **The act being prepared** | **`A-4` — *"an instruction to a professional"*** **[S-K(E): §XXVI.3]**. **Not `A-5` *(delivery of a file)*, which is a separate act with a separate history — five verification rounds, four delivery declarations, zero deliveries** |
| **Who performs it** | **One natural person.** *"He does not need an entity, a bank account, a constituted body, a budget approval, a council, a vote or a document to do it"* **[S-K(E): §XXVI.1, U-PD-9]** |
| **Who may not perform it** | **The organisation.** There is no organisation capable of acting: no legal person, no mandate, no approver. **And it may not pay: `A-N16 REMAINS BLOCKED` and `PP-04` — *"A payment made informally is not a mandate; it is an unauthorised payment"*** **[S-K(E): §XXVI.1]** |
| **Who the professional is** | **Qualified Indian corporate and tax counsel**, with a chartered accountant, an insurance broker and a labour/safeguarding specialist engaged as the questions require. **[S-F:1878]** names the owner of `SR-01` as **Founder/CEO**; **[S-F:1881]** names **National Legal + Finance + CEO** for `SR-04`; **[S-F:1975 SR-98]** requires **11 pre-pilot validations across 8 professional categories**, commissioned *"as one engagement where possible"* |
| **What the instruction must not do** | **It must not ask *"which option is correct."* The question the corpus has asked since v1.3-F is: *"Nothing is constituted. Here is what the founder intends and here are the ten candidate structures. What should be created, in India, for these four pillars, and what are the consequences of each?"*** **[S-K(E): §XXVI.1, U-PD-15]** |
| **What this brief adds to the corpus** | **Four things, none of them architectural: **(1)** the CEA's economic questions translated into professional questions with evidence requirements; **(2)** the separation of entity-dependent from entity-independent questions; **(3)** eight economic criteria absent from the existing **FC-01 … FC-33** matrix, surfaced for counsel to score; **(4)** a controlled answer format so that a reply can be filed as governance evidence** |

## II.4 The terminal rule of this brief

**[P]** Mandate §32, adopted verbatim in substance.

> ### **IF EVIDENCE IS MISSING, THIS BRIEF MARKS IT MISSING. IF AUTHORITY IS MISSING, IT MARKS IT BLOCKED. IF COUNSEL MUST ANSWER, IT MAKES IT A COUNSEL QUESTION. IF THE FOUNDER MUST DECIDE, IT MAKES IT A FOUNDER DECISION. IF GOVERNANCE MUST DECIDE, IT MAKES IT A GOVERNANCE DECISION. IF AN ENTITY MUST EXIST FIRST, IT MARKS ENTITY DEPENDENCY. AND WHERE THE CURRENT ARCHITECTURE CONFLICTS WITH LAW, IT SURFACES THE CONFLICT — IT DOES NOT SILENTLY REWRITE THE ARCHITECTURE.**
>
> **No follow-up architecture is created by this brief because anything remains unresolved. Unresolved is the expected state, and it is the state the register records.**

## III.2 Five errata and reconciliations against the CEA, recorded here and not applied there

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` for both. **The CEA is committed at `76a30b3` and is referenced by that hash. It is not modified. Both errors are recorded of record here, in the same manner as the CEA's own `CR-8` and `CR-9` corrections, so that neither is inherited by any later work.**

| # | **Erratum** | **What the CEA says** | **What the source says** | **Treatment** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **ER-1** | **`H-Q17` is mis-cited in the CEA at nine locations** | The CEA uses **`H-Q17`** as the label for the question *"which of `C_L1-a`, `C_L1-b` or `C_L1-c` funds the L2 layer?"* — at **§II.1 `MD-04`**, **§VI.2 flow C**, **§XII.3** *(a headline block)*, **§XII.4 the `L2` row**, **§XVIII.2 `F-10`**, **§XX.3 `GF-5`**, **§XX.4 `GD-02`**, **§XXXI.1 `ID-10`** and **§XXXII.1 row 9**. **The §XX.4 use is correct in form — it writes *"(`H-Q17`)"* as a gloss on `GD-02` — but it propagates the same mis-identification** | **The corpus's `H-Q17` is a different question: *"Can Sewak Sathi pay Krytos?"*** — status `UNKNOWN`, `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`, sharpened by the delta audit: *"Paying an unconstituted construct is not possible"* **[S-K(E):854]** | **The L2 funding question is properly **`GD-02`**, which the CEA also assigns. **`H-Q17` retains its corpus meaning throughout this brief and appears at **CQ-31**. The CEA's use of the label is a citation error and is not to be inherited. **Both questions remain open; nothing turns on the error except provenance**** |
| **ER-2** | **`PP-04` is characterised loosely in CEA §XXIX.1 rule 5 and `PVR-21`** | The CEA states that **`PP-04`** *"restricts instruction to the founder as a natural person"* | **`PP-04` is a pilot-protection prohibition on inferring **bank authority** from activity, and its operative sentence is *"A payment made informally is not a mandate; it is an unauthorised payment"*** **[S-J:1679]**. **The proposition the CEA wanted — that the organisation may not pay for the instruction — is stated in the delta audit's own words: *"The organisation may not pay: `A-N16 REMAINS BLOCKED`, and `PP-04`"*** **[S-K(E): §XXVI.1]** | **The CEA's conclusion is correct and is supported by the delta audit's own sentence. **Its characterisation of `PP-04` as a restriction on *who may instruct* is imprecise: the restriction on who may instruct comes from **U-PD-9** *(one natural person can perform the act)* and from the absence of any body capable of acting. **This brief cites both correctly and separately**** |

| **ER-3** | **`priority 9` does not exist in the source waterfall** | The CEA refers to **`priority 9`** for residual surplus / founder upside at six places — **§XV.3 SEP-4**, **§XV.5** *(three cells)*, **§XVI.3 `CF-16`**, **§XXII.2 AU-11** and **§XXIX.3 PVR-09** | **The source waterfall has exactly eight priorities, and residual surplus / founder upside is **priority 8**: *"8. RESIDUAL SURPLUS / FOUNDER UPSIDE"* **[appendix.txt:6301–6321]**, defined at **[appendix.txt:6362–6363]** as *"Remaining surplus after priority obligations and caps as defined by approved structure"*. **The CEA itself is correct at §VI.1 and §XIV.2, which both say priority 8** | **`priority 8` is the correct identifier and is used throughout this brief. **The CEA's §X.2 comparison table legitimately uses positions 9 and 10 — those are the **task's candidate order**, not the source's, and reinvestment genuinely has no position in the source (**CEA X.7, GD-07**). Nothing in the architecture turns on the error: **`GC-01`, `CF-16`, `PVR-09`, `AU-11` and `GD-08` all concern the same residual claim whichever number is attached to it. It is recorded because provenance is the discipline, not because the substance changes**** |

| **ER-4** | **`two impossible` and three rows marked `IMPOSSIBLE`** | The CEA's headline at **§XV.4** reads *"TEN PAIRINGS EXAMINED. ZERO PERMITTED TODAY. TWO ARE NOT MERELY UNAPPROVED — THEY ARE IMPOSSIBLE, BECAUSE ONE SIDE DOES NOT EXIST"* — while the table above it marks **three** directional rows `IMPOSSIBLE`: **Sewak Sathi → Krytos**, **Krytos → Sewak Sathi**, and **Krytos → founder** | **The two counts are reconcilable. **The three rows resolve to two impossible *pairings*: **`Krytos ↔ Sewak Sathi`** *(both directions, because one side has no legal personality — **FA-4**) and **`Krytos → founder`** *(no entity, no ownership document, no distributable surplus, no tax characterisation and no authority)*. **The third row of the `Krytos ↔ founder` pairing — **founder → Krytos** — is separately `NO INSTRUMENT EXISTS`, because **FA-6** is a future intention and no share, deed, assignment or valuation exists** | **This brief states **ten pairings · zero permissible · two impossible**, as mandate §13 requires, and identifies the two as **`Krytos ↔ Sewak Sathi`** and **`Krytos → founder`** at §XIII.4. **Nothing is lost: all three directional rows are reproduced. The reconciliation matters because a reader relying on the headline alone would under-count the flows that cannot occur even in principle**** |

| **ER-5** | **The CEA's `AC-1 … AC-5` collide with a corpus identifier series** | The CEA coins **`AC-1 … AC-5`** for its **accrual rules** — *"an earned claim that cannot be paid is recorded, aged, visible to the person it belongs to, and settled at priority 1"* — and cites them at **§XI.6**, **§XVIII.2 `F-03`**, **§XXI.2 `REC-09`** and **§XXIX.3 `PVR-04`** | **The corpus already uses `AC-1 … AC-11` for an entirely different series: **[S-C:1341]** — **`AC-1` *Founder worship***, and the eleven anti-personality-cult controls that follow. **The CEA fixed exactly this class of collision for three other prefixes — `SC-` → `PSC-`, `FC-` → `PFC-`, `M-` → `PM-` — and did not fix `AC-`** | **This brief does not inherit the collision. **Its own accounting rules are **`ACR-1 … ACR-5`** (**§XV.1**), and every reference to the accrual rules is written **"CEA `AC-1 … AC-5`"** so that the prefix is never free-standing. **The CEA is not modified. The full collision register — eight prefixes, not one — is at **§IV.5**** |

## III.3 The CEA's own verdict, preserved unchanged

> ### **`CURRENT ECONOMIC ARCHITECTURE: DESIGNED, NOT DEPLOYABLE.`**
>
> ***"`DESIGNED` because every component the task requires exists, is labelled, is sourced, and is internally consistent. `NOT DEPLOYABLE` because zero of sixteen economic acts can be executed: there is no legal person (`SR-01`), no bank mandate (`A-N16`, 0 of 5), no approver for thirteen of sixteen acts (`GF-1`), no compensation formula (`GF-3`), no accounting basis (`RR-1`), no records (`F-17`), no funding source for eight of fourteen map rows (`FM-1`), no counterparty for two of ten transfer pairings (`XV.4`), and six failure modes already open (`XVIII.4`). The gap between the two words is not a defect in the design. It is the design's finding."***
>
> **[CEA: §XXXII.2 field 8]** — **This brief exists to close that gap by professional work, and by nothing else.**

## IV.2 The rules that govern how evidence is used here

| Rule | **Content** | **Origin** |
|---|---|---|
| **EB-1** | **No source, no fact, no closure.** A proposition with no located source is not stated | **Rule J-31** |
| **EB-2** | **A request is not evidence. A receipt is not acceptance. Authenticity is not correctness. An assertion is not a document. A reference is not supply. Silence is not absence-of-problem** | **Rules K-1 … K-5** |
| **EB-3** | **No authority by repetition, and no authority by disclosure.** A proposition stated in six instruments is not thereby established; a person identifying themselves in a role does not thereby hold it | **Rules K-8, DA-30** |
| **EB-4** | **No architectural closure by drafting.** Producing a document is not performing an act | **Rule K-9; U-PD-16** |
| **EB-5** | **Do not silently correct a source.** Conflicts are preserved with both texts and both citations | **Rules DA-15, DA-16, EA-7** |
| **EB-6** | **Source terminology is preserved.** *"Virtual Brain"*, *"ecosystem head"*, *"owns and controls"*, *"organisation service fee"*, *"wage-protection buffer"*, *"verified work"* are used as their authors used them | **Rule DA-4, CEA EA-6** |
| **EB-7** | **Where an identifier cannot be located in the supplied corpus, this brief says `SOURCE LOCATION NOT VERIFIED` rather than guessing** | **Mandate §23** |
| **EB-8** | **No number is invented, and no historical figure is restated.** Where a statutory number must be discussed it is marked **`CURRENT-LAW PROFESSIONAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED`** and is not treated as an architectural assumption | **Mandate §24; DA-10; EA-9, EA-10** |

## IV.3 The seven evidence tags

**[P]** Mandate §22. **Every factual statement in this brief carries one. No tag is ever upgraded: `ASSERTION → FACT`, `PROPOSAL → DECISION` and `QUESTION → ANSWER` are the three prohibited migrations, and each is a defect if it occurs.**

| Tag | **Meaning** | **What it may never become without new evidence** |
|---|---|---|
| **`[SOURCE]`** | **Located in the current corpus at a named instrument and section, table or row** | — |
| **`[FOUNDER ASSERTION]`** | **A statement by the founder, at evidence level `E1`. Establishes intent and factual status only** | **A legal fact, an ownership fact, a capacity, an authority or a jurisdiction** |
| **`[PROFESSIONAL QUESTION]`** | **A question formulated for a named discipline** | **An answer, an assumption or a design input** |
| **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** | **A proposition that cannot be stated until a written professional opinion is held** | **A finding, a permission or a conclusion** |
| **`[UNKNOWN]`** | **The corpus is silent, or the fact does not exist. **Silence is not evidence of absence, and absence of evidence is not evidence of a negative**** | **A negative finding, unless an express negative exists — as `FA-4` and `FA-5` are** |
| **`[PROPOSED]`** | **A design proposal made by an instrument and not yet approved** | **A decision, an authority or an operative rule** |
| **`[PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION]`** | **A proposition that must not be assumed by anyone, at any stage, for any reason** | **Anything. **The five forbidden assumptions and the nineteen prohibited declarations remain prohibited**** |

## IV.4 The seven founder declarations, reproduced as assertions

**[P]** `[FOUNDER ASSERTION]` — evidence level `E1`; **FA-4** and **FA-5** are **express negatives** at `PRS-3 NONE`, not silences **[S-K(E): §II.3, 172]**. **Each is stated with the conclusion it may not support, so that no reader can mistake the second column for the fourth.**

| # | **The declaration, in the founder's own terms** | **What it establishes** | **What it does not establish** | **The prohibited conversion** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **FA-1** | **Krytos is intended to be the Virtual Brain / ecosystem head of the founder's projects** | **An intended functional and architectural role** | **A legal person, a corporate name, a registered entity, a governance layer** | `[PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION]` — **that "Virtual Brain" denotes a legal person, or that "ecosystem head" denotes a legally recognised parent** |
| **FA-2** | **Krytos is intended to be the ecosystem head** | **An intended position in an intended hierarchy** | **A parent/subsidiary relationship, a holding structure, a direction with legal authority** | `[PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION]` — **that Sewak Sathi is a subsidiary, or is legally subordinate** |
| **FA-3** | **India is intended as the governing jurisdiction** | **A stated intention as to future forum — and, per the delta audit, *"an asserted jurisdiction to scope opinions against"* at `P0-B`** | **A completed registration, a choice of law already made, an address, a regulator, a tax residency** | `[PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION]` — **that Indian law already applies to a body that does not exist, or that any filing has been made** |
| **FA-4** | **No formal Krytos constitutive or governing document has yet been established in the Sewak Sathi evidence corpus** | **An express negative — the most consequential fact in this brief** | **Nothing. **It is an absence, and every capacity depends on the thing that is absent**** | `[PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION]` — **that Krytos is already constituted** |
| **FA-5** | **No legal work establishing Krytos's legal form has yet been completed** | **An express negative** | **That legal work is unnecessary, or that any structure is compliant** | `[PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION]` — **that the current entity structure is compliant, or that a structure exists to be compliant** |
| **FA-6** | **Krytos is intended to be controlled and owned by the founder** | **A forward-looking intention, in the future tense** | **Present ownership, a shareholding, a membership, a trust deed, a directorship, signatory authority, fiduciary identity** | `[PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION]` — **that the founder is legally the shareholder, or that any upside is distributable to him** |
| **FA-7** | **The current architecture is being designed now** | **A statement about the design activity** | **Authority to make legal determinations, or approval of anything designed** | `[PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION]` — **that designing a structure constitutes deciding it, or that a design is an approval** |

| Rule | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **FA-R1** | **No declaration may be converted into legal-person status, ownership proof, corporate control, fiduciary identity, parent/subsidiary status, tax status, a jurisdictional conclusion or legal authority — unless supplied professional evidence establishes the proposition** *(mandate §2)* |
| **FA-R2** | **FA-1 … FA-7 are attached to the counsel instruction as the fact base** — *"with the seven founder declarations attached as the fact base"* **[S-K(E): §XXVI.1]** — and counsel must be told that two of them are express negatives and that none is a document |
| **FA-R3** | **A declaration does not confer authority on the discloser** *(DA-30)*. **The founder may instruct counsel because he is a natural person, not because he holds an office** |
| **FA-R4** | **FA-3 gives counsel a jurisdiction to scope opinions against. It does not give the organisation a jurisdiction it is registered in** |

# PART V — ID-1 DECISION BOUNDARY

## V.1 What `ID-1` is

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **§XXXI.1 ID-1**; **[S-K(E): §XXVI.1]**; **[S-F:175, 604, 1878]**.

| Element | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **The step** | **`ID-1` — instruction of qualified Indian corporate and tax counsel on `SR-01` / `LDR-U08`** |
| **What it unlocks** | **Everything downstream. *"Nineteen of twenty-two PVR items depend on it"* **[CEA §XXIX.4]**; *"sixteen of eighteen dependency steps sit behind it"*** |
| **What it depends on** | **Nothing. *"It is the first act available"*** |
| **Who must act** | **The founder, as a natural person** |
| **State** | **`AVAILABLE NOW — NOT TAKEN`** |
| **Its rank in the corpus** | **`SR-01` is rank 1 of the register **[S-F:600–608]**, above `LDR-U01`, `A-N16`, `A-N10` and `LDR-U06`, and *"the root of eleven of twenty-eight dependency chains"*** **[S-K(E): §XXVI.1]** |
| **Its evidence requirement** | ***"A written decision plus counsel opinion"* **[S-F:1878]** — and **the opinion comes before the decision**** |
| **Its blocking level** | **`BLOCKER A / Tier 0`; gates `P0-A`, `P0-B`** |

## V.2 The boundary of the decision — what `ID-1` does and does not decide

**[P]** Stated as a boundary because an instruction that is too wide will be answered loosely, and an instruction that is too narrow will be answered uselessly.

| `ID-1` **decides nothing** | `ID-1` **makes decidable** |
|---|---|
| **The entity form** | The **consequences** of each candidate form, in writing, against the four pillars and the eight economic criteria at §IX.4 |
| **The Krytos relationship** | Whether any relationship is **available** in law, and in what forms — the eight questions at **CQ-28 … CQ-35** |
| **Worker classification** | The **tests** that will be applied, and the facts counsel needs in order to apply them — **SR-02**, **CQ-05 … CQ-09** |
| **The accounting basis** | Whether **collection** or **invoice** is the correct recognition point, and what the four meanings of "revenue" are in the chosen framework — **PVR-05** |
| **The tax position** | Which regimes are **engaged** by each candidate form, and what each would require — Part XIV |
| **The insurance position** | What cover is **available** for each activity class, and what is uninsurable — **L1 … L15**, Part XVI |
| **Any person's liability** | The **analysis** of exposure for each of the four control roles, per classification and per form — Part XVII |
| **`GC-01`** | The **terms of the conflict**, so that a human can decide it: whether priorities 4 and 8 survive in a not-for-profit form, are amended by Tier-1 process, or are referred to a separate commercial entity |
| **Any number** | Nothing. **No number is decided, proposed, estimated or implied by `ID-1` or by its answers** |

## V.3 The three classes of question, and the discipline that separates them

**[P]** Mandate §5 requires ten classes. **They are reduced here to three gates that every question in Part XIX passes through, and the ten classes are the gate's outputs.**

| Gate | **Test** | **Output classes** |
|---|---|---|
| **GATE 1 — Is the question answerable on facts that exist today?** | If a question needs only the facts in this brief and the corpus, it is answerable now | **① Answerable now** |
| **GATE 2 — Does the answer change with the entity form?** | If yes, the question is entity-dependent and **must not be answered before `SR-01`** — an answer given now would be an answer about a body that does not exist | **② Requires entity selection · ③ Requires constitutive documents · ⑩ Cannot yet be answered because a necessary fact or legal structure does not exist** |
| **GATE 3 — Which discipline holds the answer?** | Law, tax, accounting, labour, insurance, or fact | **④ Tax/accounting · ⑤ Labour/employment · ⑥ Insurance/liability · ⑦ Factual evidence · ⑧ Founder decision · ⑨ Governance decision** |

| Class | **Definition** | **Count in Part XIX — computed, not estimated** |
|---|---|---|
| **① Answerable now** | A professional can answer it on the facts disclosed, without an entity | **`1`** primary · **`0`** secondary · **`1`** total |
| **② Requires entity selection** | The answer differs by form, and no form is selected | **`7`** primary · **`15`** secondary · **`22`** total |
| **③ Requires constitutive documents** | The answer requires a document that does not exist and must be created, not found | **`5`** primary · **`11`** secondary · **`16`** total |
| **④ Requires tax or accounting analysis** | A regime or a framework must be applied | **`8`** primary · **`12`** secondary · **`20`** total |
| **⑤ Requires labour or employment analysis** | A classification or a wage obligation must be determined | **`2`** primary · **`5`** secondary · **`7`** total |
| **⑥ Requires insurance or liability analysis** | Cover or exposure must be assessed | **`6`** primary · **`6`** secondary · **`12`** total |
| **⑦ Requires factual evidence** | A fact must be established before any analysis | **`1`** primary · **`5`** secondary · **`6`** total |
| **⑧ Requires founder decision** | Only the founder can decide, and in several cases only as a natural person | **`0`** primary · **`6`** secondary · **`6`** total |
| **⑨ Requires governance decision** | A governance layer must decide — **and thirteen of sixteen economic acts currently have no approver** | **`4`** primary · **`12`** secondary · **`16`** total |
| **⑩ Cannot yet be answered** | A necessary fact or legal structure does not exist | **`1`** primary · **`7`** secondary · **`8`** total |
| **Sum** | **Thirty-five questions are registered at Part XIX** | **35 primary classifications · 79 secondary · 114 in total. **`EVERY QUESTION CARRIES EXACTLY ONE PRIMARY CLASS; SECONDARY CLASSES ARE ADDITIONAL ROUTINGS, NOT ADDITIONAL QUESTIONS`** |

> ### **`THE CLASSES OVERLAP BY DESIGN. A QUESTION MAY REQUIRE AN ENTITY **AND** A TAX OPINION **AND** A FOUNDER DECISION. WHAT IT MAY NEVER DO IS RECEIVE AN ANSWER BEFORE ITS DEPENDENCIES EXIST.`**

## V.4 The boundary rule for this brief

| Rule | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **DBR-1** | **No question is forced to an answer because the architecture asks it** *(mandate §5)*. **An unanswered question with a named dependency is a complete output** |
| **DBR-2** | **No answer is accepted in the form "it depends" without the dependencies enumerated.** Part VI decomposes **PVR-03** into fifteen legal variables for exactly this reason; Part VII decomposes **PVR-05** into sixteen; Part VIII decomposes **PVR-22** into twenty-seven |
| **DBR-3** | **No answer format other than §XXV's controlled format is accepted from a professional**, so that a reply can be filed as governance evidence under **[S: Ch.27]** and **[S-F:1975 SR-98]** rather than read and lost |
| **DBR-4** | **A professional opinion does not close an item whose evidence requirement includes a decision.** `SR-01` requires *"a written decision **plus** counsel opinion"*. **An opinion alone leaves `SR-01` open** |
| **DBR-5** | **Nothing in the answers may be used to authorise an act.** Answers inform decisions; decisions authorise acts; and every act in **CEA §XX.2** still requires a holder that does not exist |

---

# PART VI — PVR-03: EARNED COMPENSATION AND PARTIAL PAYMENT

## VI.1 The question, in the architecture's own words

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **PVR-03**, **§XI.5 SS-0**, **§X.5 JT-1 … JT-5**, **F-07**; **[S-F:1879 SR-02]**; **[S-A: §9.1 L7]**.

> ### **`PVR-03 — IS PARTIAL PAYMENT OF EARNED COMPENSATION LAWFUL?`**
>
> **The CEA's own gate, verbatim: *"Before any pro-rata reduction is applied, confirm that partial payment is lawful. If a minimum-wage or timely-payment obligation makes partial payment unlawful outright, the correct action is not reduction but suspension of the work — and the suspension is recorded, published and escalated in the same period."*** **[CEA §XI.5 SS-0]**
>
> **`SS-0` is not one step in the shortfall rule. It is the gate on the whole rule, and it is a legal question that this architecture cannot answer.**

| Why it matters | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **1** | **Priority 1 of the waterfall is worker compensation, and the corpus makes it non-suspendable: `[S-D: INV-2]`.** A rule that cannot be suspended and a shortfall that cannot lawfully be partially discharged are in direct conflict, and the conflict is live the first time a collection falls short |
| **2** | **`[S-K(E): Finding 2]` states it plainly: *"Priority 1 is a legal obligation the organisation cannot currently discharge."*** |
| **3** | **The CEA's accrual (`AC-1 … AC-5`) records an unpaid earned claim. Whether that record is a debt, a breach, a permissible deferral or a nullity is a legal question — `PVR-04` — and it cannot be answered separately from this one** |
| **4** | **`D-2` prohibits recruiting anyone on a compensation expectation the waterfall cannot deliver in the weak month.** If partial payment is unlawful, the weak-month disclosure changes character entirely: it becomes *"work will be suspended"*, not *"you will be paid proportionally"* |
| **5** | **Every one of the four control roles and seven of the eleven roles is currently unpaid. Whether that is lawful, and for how long, is `PVR-22` and `D-8` — and the two questions share every variable in §VI.3** |

## VI.2 Why "it depends" is not an acceptable answer, and what replaces it

**[P]** Mandate §6: *"Do NOT answer with a generic 'It depends.' Instead decompose the question into the actual legal variables that determine the answer."* **The decomposition follows. Each variable is a question counsel must answer, and each is marked with what the corpus already constrains and what is `[UNKNOWN]`.**

| # | **Legal variable** | **What must be determined** | **What the corpus already fixes** | **State** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PLV-01** | **The nature of the worker relationship** | Is the relationship employment, a contract for services, a contract of service, a voluntary association, an office, an agency, a partnership, or a category Indian law recognises that this architecture has no word for? | **`LDR-U09` / `SR-02` `UNRESOLVED`, dependent on `SR-01`. `PP-07`: *"Engaging a person does not classify them; it exposes whoever engaged them"*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** |
| **PLV-02** | **Status: employee, contractor, volunteer, office-holder or other** | Which of the eleven role classes falls into which status — and whether the answer differs for a person performing **mixed work** under `MW-1 … MW-6` | **The CEA classifies roles economically, not legally: 5 value-creating, 7 delivery-supporting, 3 mission-only, 4 prohibited from paid ventures. **An economic class is not a legal status and must not be treated as one*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — per role, per person** |
| **PLV-03** | **When compensation becomes "earned"** | On performance of the work, on verification of the work, on acceptance by the customer, on collection from the customer, or on some other event? | **The architecture says *verified work*: `EL-1`, `EL-2`, `RT-02`. **Verification is an internal act. Whether an internal act can be the legal trigger for an entitlement is a question, not an answer*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** |
| **PLV-04** | **Whether the organisation may defer payment at all** | If deferral is permitted, on what conditions, with what consent, for what maximum period, and with what record | **`AC-1 … AC-5` assume deferral is possible and require it to be recorded, aged, visible and settled at priority 1. **`AC-3` already concedes that until a legal person exists an accrual is *"a moral record and not a debt"*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — `PVR-04`** |
| **PLV-05** | **Whether partial payment constitutes lawful discharge** | Does paying part extinguish part of the obligation, leave the whole obligation alive, or create a fresh exposure? Does it require the worker's consent, and is that consent meaningful when the alternative is nothing? | **`SS-1` assumes pro-rata reduction is a lawful discharge of the period, with the balance accruing. **That assumption is the whole subject of this question*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — THE CENTRAL VARIABLE** |
| **PLV-06** | **Wage and payment-timing rules, if applicable** | Which instruments apply, to whom, at what interval, with what penalty, and whether they apply at all to the relationship as classified | **The corpus requires the interval to be set — `MD-02` records the cadence as a variable with *"National approval — it is a member-facing promise"*. **No interval is stated anywhere and none may be invented: `V-01`, mandate §24**** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` + **`CURRENT-LAW PROFESSIONAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED`** for every statutory number** |
| **PLV-07** | **Contractual obligations** | What a written or oral engagement creates, and whether an engagement made before an entity exists binds anyone — and if so, whom | **No contract exists, no entity exists to make one, and `FC-08` is `PV` for every candidate form. **CEA `XV.4`: contracting requires `SR-01`*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** |
| **PLV-08** | **Statutory obligations attaching to the payment itself** | Withholding, contributions, reporting, and whether an undischarged withholding liability may sit in an account while a net amount is paid | **CEA `JT-2` raises exactly this and refuses to answer it: *"whether it is lawful to pay a net amount while an undischarged withholding liability sits in the account is a question for counsel and a CA, not for an architecture"*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — `PVR-07`** |
| **PLV-09** | **Treatment of unpaid accrued amounts** | Debt, damages, a contingent liability, a prohibited deduction, or nothing at all — and against whom, when no legal person exists | **`AC-3`, `PVR-04`. **CEA `REC-09` records the accrual with eight fields; a record is not a legal characterisation*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** |
| **PLV-10** | **Treatment of disputed amounts** | Whether a dispute suspends the obligation, whether it must be paid pending resolution, and what process is required | **CEA `DR-5` includes `DISPUTED` as a closed-set status; **Part XXI.3's grievance system** requires a route with no economic consequence. **The legal effect of a dispute is unstated*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** |
| **PLV-11** | **Treatment of insufficient revenue** | Whether inability to pay is a defence, a mitigating factor, irrelevant, or an insolvency trigger | **CEA `F-01`, `F-07`, `XIV.4`: at zero collection *"the operative control is runway, not priority"*. **The architecture describes the situation; it does not know its legal character*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** |
| **PLV-12** | **Treatment of voluntary or non-guaranteed compensation** | Whether a payment described as voluntary, discretionary or subject to availability is enforceable, and whether describing it that way is itself a disclosure or a misrepresentation | **CEA `VB-1 … VB-6`, `D-4`, `EL-3`. **`D-2` requires the weak-month reality to be disclosed at recruitment — which presupposes that the disclosure is legally effective. Whether it is, is unstated*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** |
| **PLV-13** | **Consequences of promising compensation before revenue exists** | Misrepresentation, unfair labour practice, unlawful inducement, or nothing — and whether the consequence attaches to the organisation, to the person who made the promise, or to both | **Absolute rule 2, `[S-B: §12.6]`: *"Compensation is never recruited on before it is collectable."* **The architecture prohibits the act; it does not know the penalty for it. `PVR-22` is the personal limb of this variable*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]`** |
| **PLV-14** | **Recordkeeping requirements** | What must be kept, in what form, for how long, by whom, and whether a paper record satisfies the requirement | **CEA Part XXI specifies 17 record types × 8 fields and expressly permits paper (`§XXI.1`). **`DR-9` leaves retention open: `PVR-20`, `SR-06` *"a schedule with counsel-set periods"*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — and **answerable in part before `SR-01`**** |
| **PLV-15** | **Tax consequences of partial or deferred payment** | When the taxable event occurs, whose obligation it is, and whether an accrual creates a tax position before a payment does | **CEA `REC-08` requires gross, deduction and net as separate fields and marks the split `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION`** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — `PVR-07`, Part XIV** |
| **PLV-16** | **Whether the answer differs by legal structure** | Every one of PLV-01 … PLV-15 may resolve differently for a company, a society, a trust, a Section 8 entity, an LLP, a partnership, a proprietorship, an unincorporated network and a hybrid | **`[S-F:1879 SR-02]` records the dependency explicitly: **`SR-02` depends on `SR-01`**. `[S-G] FC-05` *"Worker compensation"* is `PV` for **all ten** options — no corpus statement differentiates the forms*** | **`[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — **AND THIS IS WHY PVR-03 IS PARTLY ENTITY-DEPENDENT EVEN THOUGH IT IS ALSO PARTLY ANSWERABLE NOW**** |

## VI.3 The part that is answerable before `SR-01`, and the part that is not

**[P]** The CEA placed **PVR-03** among the three questions answerable without an entity. **That placement is correct but incomplete, and the correction is stated here rather than left for counsel to discover.**

| Limb | **Answerable before entity selection?** | **Why** |
|---|---|---|
| **PLV-13 — the consequence of promising compensation before revenue exists** | **`YES`** | It concerns conduct by a natural person toward other natural persons. **The exposure does not wait for an entity, and `PP-07` says so in the corpus's own words: *"Engaging a person does not classify them; it exposes whoever engaged them"*** |
| **PLV-12 — whether a "voluntary / subject to availability" description is effective disclosure or misrepresentation** | **`YES`** | Same reasoning. **This is the limb that governs what may be said to the eleven people already involved, today** |
| **PLV-14 — what records must be kept of work and payment** | **`PARTLY`** | The general obligation to keep records of work performed and money owed does not depend on the form; **the retention period and the statutory form do — `SR-06`, `DR-9`** |
| **PLV-05 — whether partial payment is a lawful discharge** | **`NO`** | It depends on PLV-01 and PLV-02, which depend on `SR-02`, which depends on `SR-01` **[S-F:1879]** |
| **PLV-06 — wage and payment-timing rules** | **`NO`** | Applicability turns on classification and on the form of the payer |
| **PLV-08, PLV-09, PLV-10, PLV-11, PLV-15** | **`NO`** | Each requires an obligor. **There is none** |
| **PLV-16 — the structural differential** | **`NO — BY DEFINITION`** | This is the question that makes `SR-01` rank 1 |

> ### **`THREE LIMBS OF PVR-03 ARE ANSWERABLE TODAY AND THEY ARE THE THREE THAT PROTECT PEOPLE WHO ARE ALREADY EXPOSED. THE REST WAIT ON `SR-01`, AND WAITING IS NOT A REASON TO SAY NOTHING TO THE PEOPLE CONCERNED.`**

## VI.4 What the architecture already constrains, and counsel must design inside

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — these are **not** questions. They are settled architectural positions that any answer must respect, or must expressly identify as unlawful, in which case **the conflict is surfaced and the architecture is amended by a human — never silently rewritten** *(mandate §32)*.

| Constraint | **Content** | **If counsel finds it unlawful** |
|---|---|---|
| **PVC-01** | **Priority 1 is worker compensation, and it is non-suspendable — `[S-D: INV-2]`. The waterfall is `APPROVED ARCHITECTURE` and a reserved matter** | **Surface the conflict. Amendment requires a Tier-1 process: National approval, professional validation and the founder's formal amendment — `GD-14`. **A professional opinion does not amend an approved architecture**** |
| **PVC-02** | **Priorities 1 and 2 form one indivisible joint tier in a weak period — `JT-1 … JT-5`** | **If withholding cannot lawfully be deferred while a net amount is paid, `JT-2` fails and the joint tier must be re-specified** |
| **PVC-03** | **Statutory dues are never reduced by any internal rule — `JT-3`** | **Confirmatory. Any answer that permits reduction of a statutory obligation is refused on the architecture's own terms** |
| **PVC-04** | **Shortfall is shared pro rata to verified work value; no role-holder may exclude themselves and still approve it — `SS-1`, `SS-2`** | **If pro-rata reduction is unlawful, `SS-0`'s alternative operates: suspension of work, recorded, published and escalated in the same period** |
| **PVC-05** | **Accrued claims are recorded, aged, visible to the person they belong to, and settled at priority 1 — `AC-1 … AC-5`** | **If an accrual is a debt, its character must be disclosed to the person, and `AC-3`'s "moral record" description must be withdrawn** |
| **PVC-06** | **No member funds operations personally, ever — `D-3`, absolute rule 1, `X-8`** | **Non-negotiable. Any answer whose practical effect is to require a member to fund operations is refused** |
| **PVC-07** | **The formula must exist in writing and be published before anyone is recruited on it — `D-1`, `F-06`** | **Non-negotiable, and currently unsatisfied: `[S-B: PC-01]` *"the formula does not exist"*. **This is a present-state finding, not a future risk**** |
| **PVC-08** | **Nothing is computed from any count of people — `MLM-1`, `AG-06`, `J-12`** | **Permanent, no approval route. Any compensation structure counsel proposes that varies with headcount is refused before it is costed** |
| **PVC-09** | **A leader differential must be bounded or capped, justified by verified leadership work, and may never be a percentage of team revenue — `MLM-2`, `LD-1 … LD-8`** | **Permanent. **`SR-15` / `LDR-U02` records the multiplier as *"EXPLICITLY UNRESOLVED — pilot measures it"*, Tier 2, *"do not decide before measurement"*** |
| **PVC-10** | **Four roles may not participate in paid ventures at all — Funds, HR & Verification, Safety/Protection, Volunteer Manager (`COI-1 … COI-4`)** | **If any of the four must be paid, or must be an employee, the segregation design changes and Part XVII's exposure analysis changes with it** |

## VI.5 The consequence tree — what each possible answer does to the architecture

**[P]** Stated in advance so that counsel's answer can be applied mechanically, and so that no answer is greeted with improvisation. **No branch is preferred.**

| Branch | **If the answer is…** | **Then the architecture…** | **And the dependency is…** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Partial payment of earned compensation is lawful, on conditions** | **`SS-0 … SS-6` operate as designed, with counsel's conditions added as gates. `AC-1 … AC-5` operate. `D-2` disclosure takes the form *"in a weak month you will be paid proportionally and the balance accrues"*** | **The conditions become part of `REC-08` and `REC-09` and are auditable under `AU-3`** |
| **B** | **Partial payment is unlawful; deferral is lawful with consent** | **`SS-1` is replaced by a documented deferral with consent, and the consent's validity becomes a controlled question — is consent meaningful when the alternative is nothing? `AC-1 … AC-5` operate unchanged** | **A consent record joins Part XXI's record set, and `VB-2`'s no-consequence principle extends to refusing a deferral** |
| **C** | **Both partial payment and deferral are unlawful; the obligation is absolute** | **`SS-0`'s alternative becomes the only route: **work is suspended before it is underpaid**. `HG-4` gains a pre-condition — no work may be authorised whose compensation cannot be met in full. `LC-2` tightens correspondingly** | **The pre-authorisation gate becomes the primary control, and the shortfall rule becomes a dead letter that must be removed by amendment rather than left unenforceable** |
| **D** | **The answer depends entirely on classification** | **Nothing in Part XI of the CEA may operate until `SR-02` is determined, and `SR-02` until `SR-01`. **The architecture's compensation limb is frozen, and the freeze must be disclosed to every person currently involved**** | **`ID-1 → ID-2 → ID-7 → ID-8` — the dependency chain in its exact order** |
| **E** | **The answer differs by legal structure** | **`SR-01` acquires an additional decision criterion: **the form's capacity to discharge priority 1 lawfully**. This is proposed as economic criterion **FC-E5** at §IX.4** | **Counsel's comparison must state, per option, whether priority 1 is dischargeable at all** |
| **F** | **The current unpaid state is itself unlawful for some or all of the eleven roles** | **This is the most consequential branch and the most likely to be overlooked, because no money has moved. **`D-8` becomes urgent, `VB-5`'s boundary must be published immediately, and the four `COI` roles are the first to cross it. `F-10` and `F-12` escalate from internal findings to legal exposures**** | **`PVR-22` — Part VIII — and the answer must be given to the people concerned, not only to the founder** |

## VI.6 Required professional output — PVR-03

**[P]** Mandate §30's controlled format. **`[TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL]` cells must be completed by counsel. This brief does not complete them, and no reader may treat a blank as a negative answer.**

```
QUESTION
  Is partial payment of earned compensation lawful? Decomposed into PLV-01 … PLV-16 (§VI.2),
  with the three entity-independent limbs (PLV-12, PLV-13, PLV-14) answerable immediately
  and the remainder dependent on SR-02 / SR-01.

PROFESSIONAL DISCIPLINE
  Labour and employment counsel (lead) · chartered accountant (PLV-08, PLV-15) ·
  corporate counsel (PLV-16, per candidate form)

CURRENT ARCHITECTURAL STATUS
  [SOURCE] CEA §XI.5 SS-0 … SS-6 gated on SS-0 · §X.5 JT-1 … JT-5 · §XI.6 AC-1 … AC-5 ·
  F-07 · D-1, D-2, D-3 · [S-D: INV-2] priority 1 non-suspendable ·
  [S-B: PC-01] "the formula does not exist" · [S-F:1879] SR-02 DECISION REQUIRED,
  depends on SR-01 · [S-J:1682] PP-07

FACTS REQUIRED
  The eleven current roles and who holds them · which of the eleven are performing work now ·
  whether any person has been told anything about payment · whether any work has been verified ·
  whether any money has been received from any customer · whether any person has paid anything
  to participate · the locality and the intended operating state(s) · whether any person is a minor

DOCUMENTS REQUIRED
  Any engagement letter, message, post, notice or conversation in which compensation was
  described · any record of work performed · any record of verification · any payment record ·
  the CEA Parts X, XI, XVII, XVIII · the founder's seven declarations FA-1 … FA-7

ENTITY DEPENDENCY
  YES for PLV-01 … PLV-11, PLV-15, PLV-16 · NO for PLV-12, PLV-13 · PARTLY for PLV-14

PROFESSIONAL DETERMINATION
  [TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL]

CONDITIONS / ASSUMPTIONS
  [TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL — including every assumption made about classification,
   locality, and the absence of an entity]

CONSEQUENCE FOR CURRENT ARCHITECTURE
  Branch A, B, C, D, E or F of §VI.5 — to be identified by counsel, and applied by a human
  decision, never automatically

NEXT DEPENDENCY
  Branch A/B → REC-08, REC-09, AU-3 · Branch C → HG-4, LC-2, amendment of §XI.5 ·
  Branch D → SR-01 → SR-02 → ID-8 · Branch E → FC-E5 and the SR-01 comparison ·
  Branch F → PVR-22, D-8, VB-5, and immediate disclosure to the persons concerned
```

# PART VII — PVR-05: REVENUE RECOGNITION

## VII.1 The question, and the four things that must not be conflated

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **PVR-05**, **RR-1**, **RR-2**, **F-03**, **F-04**, **X.8**, **V-04**. **Mandate §7 requires that four distinct meanings be separated. They are separated first, because every subsequent variable depends on knowing which one is being discussed.**

> ### **`PVR-05 — IS REVENUE RECOGNISED ON COLLECTION OR ON INVOICE?`**
>
> **The CEA's own finding: *"The basis of revenue recognition — receipts or invoiced — is not decided, and it is not a technicality."* The source definition leaves it open: *"Total customer receipts or invoiced revenue before deductions, **depending on final accounting policy**."*** **[CEA RR-1]**

| # | **Meaning** | **What it is** | **What it is used for** | **What it must never be used for** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **REV-1** | **`ACCOUNTING REVENUE`** | The amount recognised under the applicable accounting framework for the period, on whatever basis that framework requires | Statutory accounts, audit, tax computation, external reporting | **Paying anyone. A recognised amount is not an available amount** |
| **REV-2** | **`CASH RECEIVED`** | Money actually collected and held | Establishing what exists to be allocated | Recognising income, or measuring performance |
| **REV-3** | **`AVAILABLE DISTRIBUTABLE / OPERATING CASH`** | Cash received, **less** what is already committed — undischarged statutory obligations, costs already incurred, restricted funds, and any amount held for another purpose | The only honest input to the waterfall's `S_0` | Describing the organisation's income |
| **REV-4** | **`INTERNAL ECONOMIC WATERFALL REVENUE`** | The figure the priority order operates on: the CEA's `V_collected`, sequenced through priorities 1 … 8 and reported as the highest solvency line reached | Priority 1 … 9 allocation, `S-1 … S-6`, member-facing reporting | Any external purpose — statutory, tax, audit, donor or customer |

| Rule | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **PRS-1** | **The four are not identical and are not interchangeable.** `REV-1` may exceed `REV-2` for a whole period; `REV-3` may be zero while `REV-2` is positive; `REV-4` is defined by the architecture and not by any framework |
| **PRS-2** | **The CEA already requires all four to be recorded separately and never used alone: `RR-2` — *"a period reports `V_invoiced` and `V_collected` and the gap between them, so that the choice cannot be made invisibly later"*** |
| **PRS-3** | **Whether `REV-4` may lawfully differ from `REV-1` is itself a question — PRV-17 — and not an assumption.** An internal allocation basis that diverges from the statutory basis may be perfectly proper, or may be a misstatement, and only a professional can say which |
| **PRS-4** | **No framework is prescribed by this brief.** Mandate §17: *"Do not prescribe an accounting standard unless the evidence/professional determination establishes applicability."* **The corpus prescribes none** |

## VII.2 The seventeen variables

**[P]** Mandate §7's list, decomposed. **Each is a question for a named discipline.**

| # | **Variable** | **What must be determined** | **Corpus state** | **Tag** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **PRV-01** | **What entity would recognise the revenue** | There is no entity. **A recognition question with no recogniser is unanswerable, and the answer changes with `SR-01`** | `[S-G:2323]` — *"no legal person to incur cost, hold an account, employ anyone or be audited"*; **FC-07** `PR` for OPT-A/B/C, `PC` for OPT-D/H | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-02** | **What type of revenue is contemplated** | Service fees, retainer fees, project fees, handling charges, digital-product sales, marketing services, consulting — **each of the seven categories may have a different recognition point** | CEA Part VII: **7 categories, 0 approved, 2 blocked at Stage 0–2, 3 constrained, 1 not admitted** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — per category |
| **PRV-03** | **Customer contract status** | Is there a contract? Who is capable of making one? What terms govern delivery, acceptance, payment and dispute? | **No contract exists; no capacity to make one (`SR-01`); **FC-08** `PV` for 8 of 10 options** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-04** | **Invoice versus receipt** | The distinction the whole question turns on: an invoice is a demand, a receipt is a fact | CEA **RR-2** records both; **`G-01` fake revenue** and **AG-03** are the gaming vectors | `[SOURCE]` for the distinction · `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` for the treatment |
| **PRV-05** | **Accrual versus cash implications** | Which basis applies, whether it may be chosen, and what the choice does to priority 1's timing | **CEA `F-04`: *"a delay is a real cost to a person without reserves"*** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-06** | **Applicable accounting framework** | Which framework applies to which candidate form, and whether any framework applies at all before registration | **The corpus prescribes none. **Mandate §17 forbids prescribing one here**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-07** | **Tax treatment of the recognised amount** | When a tax obligation arises — on invoice, on collection, or on recognition — and whether the three differ | **CEA `REC-10`, `V-10`, `PVR-06`, `PVR-07`** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` + `REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE` |
| **PRV-08** | **Indirect-tax implications** | Registration thresholds, the point of supply, invoicing requirements, and whether an unregistered body may issue a compliant invoice at all | **CEA `PVR-06`: *"A category that is lawful but unregistrable is not a category."* **Every threshold is `CURRENT-LAW PROFESSIONAL VERIFICATION REQUIRED` — no number is stated here**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-09** | **Service delivery timing** | Whether recognition follows delivery, acceptance, or the passage of a period — and what happens where delivery is partial | **CEA `REC-03` separates delivery from acceptance; **`G-08` artificial completion** is the gaming vector** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-10** | **Advance payments** | Money received before delivery: liability, income, or restricted fund — and whether it may enter the waterfall at all | **The CEA has no advance-payment rule. **This is a gap, and it is recorded as a gap rather than filled**** | `[UNKNOWN — PROFESSIONAL DETERMINATION REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-11** | **Unpaid invoices** | Ageing, provisioning, and whether an unpaid invoice may support a compensation accrual | **CEA `F-03`, `AC-5`, `REC-09`; *"verified work goes unpaid — this is the structural problem"*** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-12** | **Refunds** | Whether a refund reverses recognition, creates a liability, or is a period cost — and who authorises it | **No corpus rule. **CEA `DR-5` includes `REVERSED` as a status but does not specify refunds**** | `[UNKNOWN — PROFESSIONAL DETERMINATION REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-13** | **Disputed invoices** | Whether a dispute suspends recognition, and whether work performed under dispute is compensable | **CEA `DR-5` `DISPUTED`; `PLV-10`** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-14** | **Bad debts** | When a debt is bad, who decides, and whether a worker's accrued claim survives the write-off | **CEA `AC-4`: an accrual *"may not be extinguished by a bad month, by a departure, by a role change or by silence"* — and **`F-03` makes the organisation, not the worker, bear the default**. **Whether that is sustainable in law is unstated**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-15** | **Revenue recognition versus cash availability** | The gap between `REV-1` and `REV-3`, and what may be promised on the strength of each | **CEA `X.8`: *"A team can sit above one line and below the next"* — the six solvency lines exist precisely to keep the two apart** | `[SOURCE]` |
| **PRV-16** | **Economic waterfall treatment** | Which of `REV-1 … REV-4` is `S_0` in the CEA's computable sequence | **CEA `X.4` defines `S_0 = V_collected` — i.e. `REV-2`. **Whether `REV-3` is the correct input, given commitments already incurred, is a professional question the architecture has not resolved**** | `[PROPOSED]` + `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **PRV-17** | **Whether internal compensation revenue may differ from statutory or accounting revenue** | Whether `REV-4` may lawfully and properly diverge from `REV-1`, and if so within what limits and with what disclosure | **CEA `PRS-3`. **This is the question that decides whether the architecture's internal economics and its statutory accounts can tell different stories without one of them being wrong**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |

## VII.3 What is answerable before `SR-01`

| Limb | **Answerable now?** | **Why** |
|---|---|---|
| **PRV-04 — the invoice/receipt distinction and its internal consequences** | **`YES`** | It is a distinction about facts, not about persons. **`RR-2` already operates it, and counsel can confirm or correct the treatment without an entity** |
| **PRV-15, PRV-16 — the separation of the four meanings, and which is `S_0`** | **`YES, AS AN INTERNAL DESIGN QUESTION`** | The architecture may define its own internal allocation input, provided the divergence is disclosed and lawful — which is **PRV-17** |
| **PRV-10, PRV-12 — advances and refunds** | **`PARTLY`** | The *need* for a rule is established now; **its content depends on the framework** |
| **PRV-01 … PRV-03, PRV-05 … PRV-09, PRV-11, PRV-13, PRV-14, PRV-17** | **`NO`** | **Each requires a recogniser, a framework or a counterparty. None exists** |

## VII.4 The consequence tree

| Branch | **If the answer is…** | **Then…** |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | **Collection basis** | **`RR-1` resolves toward `REV-2`. Priority 1 is paid only from money in hand. **`F-04` shrinks and `F-03` grows: a customer's delay becomes a worker's non-payment directly, with no accrual bridge unless `AC-1 … AC-5` are lawful**** |
| **B** | **Invoice / accrual basis** | **`REV-1` governs the accounts and `REV-2` governs the cash. **The waterfall must operate on `REV-2` regardless, because an invoice cannot pay anyone — and the divergence between the accounts and the allocation becomes a permanent disclosure obligation**** |
| **C** | **Framework-dependent, and no framework applies yet** | **`RR-2` becomes the operative rule indefinitely: both figures recorded, neither used alone, the gap reported. **This is the current de facto state, and it is not a defect — it is the correct response to an unresolved basis**** |
| **D** | **Different per category** | **Part VII of the CEA acquires a per-category recognition attribute, and **`G1 … G4`** gains a recognition question at economic validation. Two categories are already blocked at Stage 0–2 and would remain so**** |
| **E** | **`REV-4` may lawfully differ from `REV-1`** | **The architecture's internal economics survive intact, with a disclosure obligation. **This is the branch the CEA implicitly assumes and never validates**** |
| **F** | **`REV-4` may not differ from `REV-1`** | **The waterfall's `S_0` becomes a statutory figure, and the CEA's `X.4` sequence must be re-specified against it — a Tier-1 amendment, `GD-14`** |

## VII.5 Required professional output — PVR-05

```
QUESTION
  Is revenue recognised on collection or on invoice? Decomposed into PRV-01 … PRV-17 (§VII.2),
  with the four meanings of "revenue" (REV-1 … REV-4) separated first and never conflated.

PROFESSIONAL DISCIPLINE
  Chartered accountant (lead) · tax counsel (PRV-07, PRV-08) · corporate counsel (PRV-01, PRV-03,
  PRV-06, per candidate form)

CURRENT ARCHITECTURAL STATUS
  [SOURCE] CEA RR-1, RR-2, RR-5, RR-6 · X.4 S_0 = V_collected · X.8 S-1 … S-6 ·
  F-03, F-04 · REC-04, REC-16 · V-04 · PVR-05 · [S-B: §12.7] the delayed-payment risk ·
  the source definition "Total customer receipts or invoiced revenue before deductions,
  depending on final accounting policy"

FACTS REQUIRED
  Whether any invoice has been issued · whether any money has been received · whether any
  advance has been taken · whether any refund has been made · the seven categories and which
  are contemplated first · the intended customer type · the intended operating state(s) ·
  whether any customer is a related party

DOCUMENTS REQUIRED
  Any invoice, quotation, receipt, message or agreement · any bank or cash record ·
  CEA Parts IX, X, XXI · the seven-category table at CEA §VII.2

ENTITY DEPENDENCY
  YES for PRV-01 … PRV-03, PRV-05 … PRV-09, PRV-11, PRV-13, PRV-14, PRV-17 ·
  NO for PRV-04, PRV-15, PRV-16 (as internal design) · PARTLY for PRV-10, PRV-12

PROFESSIONAL DETERMINATION
  [TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL — per candidate form, and per category where the answer differs]

CONDITIONS / ASSUMPTIONS
  [TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL — including the framework assumed, if any, and the effect of
   no framework applying]

CONSEQUENCE FOR CURRENT ARCHITECTURE
  Branch A, B, C, D, E or F of §VII.4 — identified by counsel, applied by human decision

NEXT DEPENDENCY
  Branch A/B → RR-1 resolved, REC-04 and REC-16 amended, F-03/F-04 re-specified ·
  Branch C → RR-2 remains operative, no amendment · Branch D → CEA Part VII gains a
  recognition attribute per category · Branch E → disclosure obligation added to REC-16 ·
  Branch F → Tier-1 amendment of X.4 (GD-14)
```

# PART VIII — PVR-22: PERSONAL LIABILITY OF THE FOUR UNPAID CONTROL ROLES

## VIII.1 The question, and the four people it is about

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **PVR-22**, **§V.2 COI-1 … COI-4**, **§XI.7 D-8**, **§XII.2 FM-2**, **VB-5**, **V-16**, **GD-10**; **[S-A: §9.1 L6, L7, L12, L13, L15]**; **[appendix.txt:6925–6948]** Diagram 20A; **[appendix.txt:6950–6958]** §20.4.

> ### **`PVR-22 — DO THE FOUR UNPAID CONTROL ROLES CARRY PERSONAL LIABILITY FOR THE PEOPLE HOLDING THEM?`**
>
> **Mandate §8: *"Do NOT assume that 'unpaid' means 'voluntary liability.'"* And: *"Do not state that these people 'are personally liable' or 'are protected' unless professional evidence establishes it."* **No such evidence exists. This Part states neither.****

| # | **Role** | **Why it may not participate in paid ventures** | **What it nonetheless handles** | **Corpus anchor** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Funds Manager** | **Segregation of duties — `COI-1`** | **Custody, receipt, recording and disbursement of every rupee; the four-hand separation; the authority limits at `[appendix.txt:6950–6958]` §20.4, which name the things the money handler may never do** | **CEA §V.2, §XII.2 row 7; Diagram 20A at `[appendix.txt:6925–6948]`** |
| **2** | **HR & Verification Manager** | **Independence — `COI-2`; may not be paid from what it verifies (`EL-2`, `EL-5`)** | **Verification of work on which priority 1 depends; personnel records; the eligibility decisions that determine who is paid** | **CEA §V.2, §XI.2, §XII.2 row 6** |
| **3** | **Safety / Protection Lead** | **`NM-3` prohibits participation in any revenue activity** | **Protection and first-response decisions; safeguarding; incident handling; the activation of an activity class whose insurability is unknown (`L4`)** | **CEA §V.2, §XII.2 row 9; [S-A: A-06 V1…V43, A-09, A-10 §9.1 L4, L8, L9, L10, L15]** |
| **4** | **Volunteer Manager** | **`NM-10` — volunteer labour may never be supplied to a third party for money** | **The assignment of people to work; the paid/volunteer boundary; the declination right (`VB-2`)** | **CEA §V.2, §XI.8, §XII.2 row 10** |

| Structural fact | **Content** | **Tag** |
|---|---|---|
| **None of the four has a funding source** | **CEA `FM-2`: *"The 4 roles that may not participate in paid ventures at all … are precisely the roles with no funding source. The architecture prohibits them from earning and does not fund them"*** | `[SOURCE]` |
| **All four are performed by people who also perform delivery work** | **There is no segregation available at eleven people: CEA `F-11` is open, and `GD-11` asks who performs the second pair of hands** | `[SOURCE]` |
| **None of the four has been told what they are accepting** | **CEA `D-8` requires *"a stated point at which a role ceases to be volunteerable"* and marks it `REQUIRES PROFESSIONAL VALIDATION` + `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION`. **`L12` asks the same question from the insurance side: *"What must a member be told, in writing and in their language, about what is and is not covered, before they take a safety role?"* — with the corpus's own reason: *"A member who believes they are covered and is not has been misled by the organisation"*** | `[SOURCE]` |
| **The organisation cannot indemnify them** | **There is no legal person to give an indemnity, no account to fund one, and no insurance to sit behind it. `A-N16 REMAINS BLOCKED`, 0 of 5** | `[SOURCE]` |

> ### **`THIS IS THE ONLY QUESTION IN THE REGISTER WHOSE ANSWER PROTECTS A PERSON RATHER THAN A STRUCTURE. IT IS ALSO THE ONE THE CEA IDENTIFIED AS THE MOST URGENT ITEM CONCERNING A PERSON: *"Four people are being asked to hold exposure, unpaid, and the architecture cannot yet tell them whether it is theirs."* [CEA §XXIX.4]**

## VIII.2 The twenty-seven dimensions counsel must examine

**[P]** Mandate §8's list, decomposed. **Every cell is a question. No cell is an answer.**

| # | **Dimension** | **What counsel must examine** | **What the corpus already fixes** | **Tag** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **LD-01** | **Role-holder status** | What a person *is* when they hold a role in an organisation that has no legal existence | **The eleven roles are specified at `[appendix.txt:1808–2230]`; the ladder at `[appendix.txt:58]`, §8A.2, §8A.3. **A specification is not a status**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-02** | **Employee status** | Whether any of the four is or becomes an employee by conduct, irrespective of any label | **`SR-02` / `LDR-U09` `UNRESOLVED`; **`PP-07`**: *"Engaging a person does not classify them; it exposes whoever engaged them"*** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-03** | **Volunteer status** | Whether "volunteer" is a legal category with defined consequences, or a description with none | **CEA `VB-1 … VB-6`, `D-4`, `D-6`; `[S-B: §16.3]`, `B-09`** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-04** | **Office-holder status** | Whether any of the four is an *office* with statutory duties attaching to the office rather than to the person | **CEA `XX.1` distinguishes `SIGNS` and `AUTHORISES` as functions; no office exists because no body exists** | `[UNKNOWN — PROFESSIONAL DETERMINATION REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-05** | **Agency** | Whether a person acting for an unconstituted body acts as its agent, as a principal, or as an agent of the founder personally | **CEA `XV.4`: two pairings are *"impossible — no counterparty"*. **Agency without a principal is the exact question**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-06** | **Fiduciary duties** | To whom a duty is owed when the beneficiary class is *"the public"* and the organisation is nobody | **`A-N14` data fiduciary `UNRESOLVED`; **`SR-05`**; the delta audit: *"the fiduciary cannot be an unconstituted body"*** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-07** | **Negligence** | The standard of care for an unpaid person performing verification, custody, safeguarding or assignment decisions | **CEA `F-17`: an unverifiable transaction is treated as unauthorised. **A standard of care is a legal question, not an internal rule**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-08** | **Statutory liability** | Which statutes attach duties directly to an individual rather than to a body — and whether they attach where the body does not exist | **CEA `REC-10` records obligations as `UNKNOWN`; `PVR-10`, `PVR-16`, `PVR-17`** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-09** | **Financial handling** | Personal exposure for custody of money held without a mandate, an account or a separation from personal funds | **`A-N16 T-5` **FAILS**: *"separation of personal and organisational funds"* — `OC-10`: 0 of 17 asset classes have a stated owner; `A-N15` custody `UNRESOLVED`. **`PP-04`: *"A payment made informally is not a mandate; it is an unauthorised payment"*** | `[SOURCE]` for the facts · `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` for the consequence |
| **LD-10** | **Safeguarding** | Personal exposure arising from safeguarding decisions, the two-adult rule, image and consent absolutes, and mandatory reporting | **`SR-31` certified safeguarding outstanding; **`P0-F`**; `[S-A: A-06 V1…V43, A-09, A-B6]`; **`L10`** mandatory reporting duties** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-11** | **Employment decisions** | Exposure for decisions about who is engaged, verified, paid, deferred or exited — taken by a person with no authority to take them | **CEA `XX.2`: `APPROVES` is `UNASSIGNED` for paying compensation; **`GF-1`**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-12** | **Privacy and data handling** | Exposure for collecting, holding or disclosing personal data where no fiduciary is appointed and no retention schedule exists | **`A-N14` `UNRESOLVED`; `A-N26` retention *"deliberately not invented"*; **`PP-05`**: collecting data *"creates the exposure"*; `[S-A:1587]` *"Regulator has no contactable person; duties unassigned"*** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-13** | **Safety and protection decisions** | Exposure for activating, directing or declining a protection intervention | **`L4`: *"Is any cover available at all for acts of 'protection' or 'first response'? If not, what is the consequence for the pillar's design?"* · **`L8`** the legal boundary of intervention · **`L15`** activities that protocol should simply prohibit · **`PP-06`**: *"Activity performed is not activity covered"*** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-14** | **Representations made to third parties** | Exposure for statements made to customers, beneficiaries, donors, officials or the public on behalf of a body that does not exist | **CEA `XI.9`, `AG-15`; **`P0-G`** the claim ladder and claim classes; `[S-E: Rule E-215]`; **`C-24`**: publishing any claim above `CL-1` is prohibited** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-15** | **Authority to sign** | Whether anyone can sign, and what a signature by a person without capacity produces | **CEA `XX.2`: `SIGNS` is `BLOCKED` in **every** act involving money; `GD-05`** | `[SOURCE]` — `BLOCKED` |
| **LD-16** | **Authority to approve** | Whether an approval given by a person with no delegated authority is effective internally, externally, or at all | **CEA `GF-1`: `APPROVES` `UNASSIGNED` in 13 of 16 acts; `LDR-U01`, `A-N19`, `LDR-U19` `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`; *"the body that would decide is itself the subject of the decision"* `[S-F:159]`** | `[SOURCE]` — `UNASSIGNED` |
| **LD-17** | **Authority to spend** | Whether spending is possible at all, and by whom | **`A-N16 REMAINS BLOCKED`; **`C-15`**: *"no external payment can lawfully or operationally be made by or to this architecture"* `[S-K(E):1599 `C-15`; [S-I: §XIII.3]]`** | `[SOURCE]` — `BLOCKED` |
| **LD-18** | **Authority to hold funds** | Whether funds may be held, where, in whose name, and with what custody instrument | **`SR-19` / `A-N15` custody of reserves and the social-impact fund `EXPLICITLY UNRESOLVED`, Tier 3, *"before reserves become material"*; **`SR-03`** the mandate document** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-19** | **Criminal exposure, where relevant** | Which acts in this architecture could engage criminal provision — custody of others' money, collection from the public, intervention, data handling, misrepresentation | **`[S-ST: Annexure B]` marks criminal/liability as a validation dimension; **`L8`** private-person intervention; **`L15`** activities protocol should prohibit. **This brief asserts no criminal exposure and excludes none**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-20** | **Civil liability** | To workers, customers, beneficiaries, donors and third parties | **`L3` public/third-party liability; **`L7`** vicarious liability by classification; `FC-28`** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-21** | **Indemnification** | Whether an unconstituted body can indemnify, whether the founder can personally, and what an indemnity from a person with no entity is worth | **No corpus instrument provides one. **CEA Part XVI provides recusal and disclosure, not indemnity**** | `[UNKNOWN — PROFESSIONAL DETERMINATION REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-22** | **Insurance** | What cover is available for each of the four roles, and whether any is available at all for protection activity | **`SR-04` requires written answers to **L1–L15**; **0 held**. **`L6`** is directly on point: *"What cover is required for officers, governors and the founder personally, given that the Council structure is not yet constituted?"* — with the corpus's reason: *"Personal exposure deters capable people from governance roles."* **`DA-13`: `"COVERAGE EXISTS"` appears nowhere as an assertion**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — **`QUESTION FOR BROKER/COUNSEL ONLY`** |
| **LD-23** | **Organisational liability** | Whether a body that does not exist can be liable, and whether liability attaches to the people who acted in its name | **CEA `XV.1`: no transfer is permissible because *"there is no legal person on at least one side of every one of those pairings"*. **The mirror image — no liability either — is not established and must not be assumed**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-24** | **Personal liability** | Whether, in the absence of a body, the person who acted is the only available defendant | **`[S-ST: §11.1]`: *"the institution's identity and its legal continuity are the same object, held by one person … there is no way to defend the institution by separating them."* **An analysis of a source statement, not a legal conclusion**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-25** | **Vicarious liability** | Whether the organisation, the founder, or a team is liable for a member's acts — and how that changes with classification | **`L7` verbatim: *"What is the organisation's potential liability for a member's acts — vicarious, principal, or otherwise — and how does that change with member classification (volunteer / stipend / contract / employee)?"* — with the corpus's own note: *"Classification is UNRESOLVED, so liability is [unresolved]"*** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-26** | **Limitation or exclusion of liability** | Whether a disclaimer, a refusal list, a scope statement or a published boundary reduces exposure — and whether it is effective against a beneficiary, a minor, or a statutory duty | **CEA `NM-1 … NM-12` and the six free categories limit **conduct**; they do not limit **liability**, and the difference is a legal question. **`L15`: *"The cheapest risk control is the activity not performed"*** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` |
| **LD-27** | **Whether liability differs by legal structure** | Every one of LD-01 … LD-26, per candidate form | **`FC-28` liability is `PV` for all ten options; **`L6`** and **`L7`** are `[V]`. **No corpus statement differentiates the forms on personal exposure**** | `[PROFESSIONAL ANSWER REQUIRED]` — **`SR-01`-DEPENDENT** |

| **Whether any money has been received or held by anyone** | **No. **`A-N16` 0 of 5; `C-19` collecting funds is prohibited; **no revenue exists**** | `[SOURCE]` |
| **Whether any personal funds have been mixed with any organisational funds** | **`[UNKNOWN]` — and **`T-5` FAILS** because separation is not established, not because mixing has been proven. **Absence of proof is not proof of absence**** | `[UNKNOWN]` |
| **Whether any protection or first-response activity has occurred** | **`P0-F` fails; **`C-21`** prohibits operating the protection function; **`PP-06`**: *"Activity performed is not activity covered."* **If it has occurred, `A-B2` applies: *"You cannot retro-insure a known event"*** | `[SOURCE]` + `[UNKNOWN]` as to fact |
| **Whether any minor has been involved in any activity** | **`C-22` prohibits it: `SR-31` certified safeguarding outstanding; `P0-F` makes *"any minor activity without certified safeguarding"* a FAIL condition** | `[SOURCE]` + `[UNKNOWN]` as to fact |
