# PART XVI — LIABILITY AND INSURANCE

## XVI.1 The framing rule, preserved verbatim

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — **[S-A: §9 chapter framing rule, line 695]**. **This brief adopts it as its own and adds nothing to it.**

> ### *"This document **does not state what insurance the organisation has, needs, or can obtain.** It states what must be established in writing, by a qualified professional, before protection activity is activated or publicised. **The distinction matters: an architecture that assumes cover exists will act as though it does.**"* **[S-A:695]**
>
> *"Each row is a **question that must be answered in writing by a qualified professional**, with the answer retained as a governance record under **[S: Ch.27]**."* **[S-A:699]**
>
> **Mandate §18 is therefore not a constraint imposed on this brief from outside. It is the corpus's own rule, restated. Nothing in this Part claims coverage, states that the four unpaid control roles *"are personally liable"*, or states that they *"are protected"*. Mandate §8, §18.**

| The three permitted labels | **Definition** | **Where each may appear** |
|---|---|---|
| **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **The corpus states a requirement, a prohibition or a register row in its own words** | **Every L row; §9.2's four rules; §9.3's eight stages; `SR-04`; `PP-06`; `A-10`; `A-11`** |
| **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** | **A question that a named professional must answer in writing, and that this brief does not answer** | **Every L row's answer; every RC row's consequence** |
| **`UNKNOWN`** | **The answer does not exist. Not *unfavourable* — absent** | **Every coverage position, every premium, every exclusion, every limit, every notification period** |

> ### **`NO ROW IN THIS PART MAY CARRY ANY OTHER LABEL. `COVERED`, `UNCOVERED`, `PROTECTED`, `EXPOSED`, `LIABLE`, `AT RISK` AND `INSURABLE` ARE ALL PROHIBITED AS CONCLUSIONS — EACH MAY APPEAR ONLY INSIDE A QUESTION.`**

## XVI.2 `L1 … L15` — the requirement-to-validate register, reproduced

**[P]** `[SOURCE REQUIREMENT]` — **[S-A:697–717 §9.1; `L1` at 703, `L15` at 717]**; **`SR-04`** — *"written answers to L1–L15"* **[S-F:1881]**; **[S-A:2340 V4]** insurance broker — *"Every row L1–L7 and L14; whether any cover exists for volunteer first-response activity, member injury, third-party injury, legal defence, cyber and trustee liability"*; **[S-B:3999 EV-5]** insurance broker — *"Whether any cover exists for venture delivery, professional indemnity on client work, member injury, third-party injury, cyber, and trustee liability — v1.3-A A-10 L1–L15"*; **`PP-06`** — insurance coverage `UNKNOWN` **[S-J:1681]**.

| ID | **The question, as the corpus puts it** | **Professional to answer** | **Why it is on the list — the corpus's own reason** | **Answer** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **L1** | **What insurance products are available and appropriate for a mass-membership voluntary organisation performing community service and emergency first response in India? What are their standard exclusions?** | **Insurance broker / adviser **`[V]`**** | ***"The organisation cannot design its protocol around cover it cannot obtain"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L2** | **What personal accident or medical-expense cover can be provided for members acting in organisational capacity, at what cost per member, and what activities are excluded?** | **Broker **`[V]`**** | ***"A member injured while helping is the most morally urgent liability the organisation has"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L3** | **What public/third-party liability cover is available for service activities, events, distributions, and emergency assistance — including injury to a person being helped and damage to property?** | **Broker **`[V]`**** | ***"Third-party claims arise from ordinary service, not only from protection activity"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L4** | **Is any cover available at all for acts of "protection" or "first response"? If not, what is the consequence for the pillar's design?** | **Broker + counsel **`[V]`**** | ***"If this class of activity is uninsurable, the activity must be redesigned or not activated (A-11)"*** | **`UNKNOWN` — AND THIS IS THE ONLY ROW WHOSE ANSWER COULD DELETE AN ARCHITECTURAL PILLAR`** |
| **L5** | **What professional indemnity is required where members give advice (legal, tax, medical-adjacent, technical, engineering, safeguarding)?** | **Broker + counsel **`[V]`**** | ***"The Specialist tier (stress-test §9) will include professionals; their advice creates exposure"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L6** | **What cover is required for officers, governors and the founder personally, given that the Council structure is not yet constituted **[S: Ch.33]**?** | **Broker + counsel **`[V]`**** | ***"Personal exposure deters capable people from governance roles"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L7** | **What is the organisation's potential liability for a member's acts — vicarious, principal, or otherwise — and how does that change with member classification (volunteer / stipend / contract / employee)?** | **Counsel **`[V]`**** | ***"Classification is UNRESOLVED **[S: Ch.33]**, so liability is currently indeterminate"*** | **`UNKNOWN — AND INDETERMINATE UNTIL `SR-02` AND `CD-01` RETURN`** |
| **L8** | **What is the legal boundary of the intervention step (**[S: Ch.16 step 7]**)? What does applicable criminal procedure permit a private person to do, and what are the consequences of exceeding it?** | **Counsel **`[V]`**** | ***"The operational boundary must be defined by counsel, not by this document"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** — and **[S-D: §36.4 `VG-1 … VG-6`]** already bars enforcement: *"local authority is not legal authority"* |
| **L9** | **What protections, if any, apply to a layperson giving emergency first aid in good faith, and what conditions attach?** | **Counsel **`[V]`**** | ***"Determines what first-aid training may lawfully cover and what members may be told"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L10** | **What mandatory reporting duties arise on learning of offences against children, women, or vulnerable persons — who must report, to whom, within what time?** | **Counsel + safeguarding professional **`[V]`**** | ***"The protocol in A-09 escalates these but does not state the legal duty"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L11** | **What permissions, notifications or registrations are required for: food distribution, health camps, ambulance-like transport, disaster response, animal handling, use of public space, and collection of donations or materials?** | **Counsel + sector regulators **`[V]`**** | ***"[S: Ch.15] already names category controls; the permissions themselves are unspecified"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L12** | **What must a member be told, in writing and in their language, about what is and is not covered, before they take a safety role?** | **Counsel + HR **`[V]`**** | ***"A member who believes they are covered and is not has been misled by the organisation"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L13** | **What records must exist to support a claim, a defence, or a regulatory question — training certificates, authorisation records, incident reports, supervision records?** | **Counsel + broker **`[V]`**** | ***"Cover is only as good as the evidence that the organisation acted properly"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L14** | **What is the incident-notification obligation to an insurer, and within what time?** | **Broker **`[V]`**** | ***"Late notification commonly voids cover"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |
| **L15** | **Does any activity contemplated (e.g. transporting an injured person, administering anything, moving a person in crisis) create liability that protocol should simply prohibit?** | **Counsel **`[V]`**** | ***"The cheapest risk control is the activity not performed"*** | **`UNKNOWN`** |

## XVI.3 The coverage confirmation rule, preserved in full

**[P]** `SOURCE REQUIREMENT` — **[S-A: §9.2, lines 719–726; rule 1 at 722]**. **All four rules are reproduced without amendment, because each is an activation prohibition and each is currently in force.**

| # | **Rule** | **Current effect** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | ***"**No written confirmation, no activation.** Protection activity may not be activated, trained for operationally, publicised, or represented to any external party until the relevant items in §9.1 are answered in writing and the answers are recorded as governance evidence."*** | **`IN FORCE`** — **0 of 15** L rows answered in writing, so **no protection activity may be activated, trained for, publicised or represented**. **`SR-31`** — certified safeguarding — is likewise `DECISION REQUIRED` |
| **2** | ***"The Public Protection Statement (A-07) may not be published before the answers to L2, L3, L4, L8 and L12 exist.** Publishing a description of protective activity without cover converts an internal doctrine into a public representation."*** | **`IN FORCE`** — **`A-07` may not be published.** This is a **`PROHIBITED ASSUMPTION`** boundary and Part XXVII lists it as a stop condition |
| **3** | ***"Every member taking a safety role receives a written cover statement** in their own language: what is covered, what is not, what they must do if something happens, and whom to call. Signed acknowledgement retained."*** | **`UNSATISFIABLE TODAY`** — the statement cannot be written because L12 is `UNKNOWN`. **No member may therefore take a safety role** |
| **4** | ***"Nothing in this framework may be represented to a member, partner, authority or the public as insurance cover** unless it is confirmed in writing by the insurer."*** | **`IN FORCE`** — and it binds **this brief**. Nothing in Parts XVI or XVII is a representation of cover |

## XVI.4 The fifteen risk classes

**[P]** Mandate §18: fifteen risk classes. **Each is derived from an L row, so that no class is invented. **Every cell in the last two columns carries one of the three permitted labels and nothing else.**

| ID | **Risk class** | **Derived from** | **Label of the requirement** | **Label of the answer** | **Consequence if the answer is adverse** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **RSK-1** | **Personal injury or medical expense of a member acting in organisational capacity** | **L2** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — the corpus calls it *"the most morally urgent liability the organisation has"* |
| **RSK-2** | **Injury to a person being helped, including during emergency assistance** | **L3** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** |
| **RSK-3** | **Damage to third-party property during service, events or distributions** | **L3** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** |
| **RSK-4** | **The uninsurability of protection and first-response activity as a class** | **L4** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION` — AND THE ONLY CLASS THAT COULD REQUIRE A PILLAR TO BE REDESIGNED OR NOT ACTIVATED (`A-11`)`** |
| **RSK-5** | **Professional advice exposure — legal, tax, medical-adjacent, technical, engineering, safeguarding** | **L5**, **[S-C:4126 HV-5]** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — and **[S-C: §47.3]** already holds that *"internal training is not legally sufficient"* |
| **RSK-6** | **Personal exposure of officers, governors and the founder** | **L6**, **[S-A:2340 V4]**, **[S-B:3999 EV-5]** — *"trustee liability"* | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — *"personal exposure deters capable people from governance roles"*, which is also the answer to why **`GD-01`** may never be filled by an invented body |
| **RSK-7** | **Vicarious, principal or other liability for a member's acts, varying with classification** | **L7**, **`SR-02`**, **`CD-01`**, **`CD-18`** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — and **indeterminate until classification is determined**, which is why **`PP-07`** is `UNKNOWN` |
| **RSK-8** | **Criminal exposure from exceeding the intervention boundary** | **L8**, **[S-D: §36.4 `VG-1 … VG-6`]**, **CR-3**, **CR-4**, **[S-A:2341 V5]** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — **`VG-1`**: *"stop-work and escalate, never enforce"*; **`VG-2`**: any instruction to detain, search, confiscate, interrogate or expel is outside every role's authority |
| **RSK-9** | **Layperson first-aid exposure, and the limits of what training may lawfully cover** | **L9** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** |
| **RSK-10** | **Failure to make a mandatory report concerning a child, a woman or a vulnerable person** | **L10**, **[S-C:4123 HV-2]**, **`A-09`**, **`SR-31`** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — and **`A-20`** makes a use-of-force or detention incident an immediate sealed-category integrity matter |
| **RSK-11** | **Acting without a required permission, notification or registration** | **L11**, **[S-B:4003 EV-9]** — *"category 7 licensing (health, safety, tax, liability)"*, **[S: Ch.15]**, **[S: Ch.17]** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — and **CEA Part VII** holds the seven categories as a closed universe with **0 approved**, which is currently the only thing preventing this class from crystallising |
| **RSK-12** | **Misrepresenting cover to a member, partner, authority or the public** | **L12**, **§9.2 rules 2 and 4**, **`A-07`**, **[S-C:4128 HV-7]** consumer / advertising counsel | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — and the corpus's own words: *"a member who believes they are covered and is not has been misled by the organisation"* |
| **RSK-13** | **Inability to evidence that the organisation acted properly when a claim, defence or regulatory question arises** | **L13**, **AH-16**, **REC-01 … REC-17**, **`DR-1 … DR-9`** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION` — AND THE ONLY CLASS WHOSE CONTROL IS AVAILABLE TODAY: the record. **CEA **`ID-13`**, this brief **§XV.4**** |
| **RSK-14** | **Late notification to an insurer** | **L14**, **§9.3 stage 5** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — *"late notification commonly voids cover"*, and **no insurer exists to notify** |
| **RSK-15** | **Liability created by an activity that protocol should simply prohibit** | **L15** | **`SOURCE REQUIREMENT`** | **`UNKNOWN`** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** — *"the cheapest risk control is the activity not performed"* |

| **Trustee, officer and director liability** | **[S-A:2340 V4]**; **[S-B:3999 EV-5]**; **L6** | **`PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** · answer **`UNKNOWN`** | **Directly entangled with **RSK-6** and with Part XVII: the four unpaid control roles would be the officers and trustees. **Whether an unpaid role-holder bears trustee or officer liability is precisely the question Part XVII refuses to answer**** |

## XVI.6 The legal support protocol, and the gap it was written to close

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — **[S-A: §9.3, lines 727–740]**; **[S-STRESS: F-10]**. **Reproduced because it is the corpus's answer to a liability question, and because CEA **`F-10`** is one of the six failure modes already open.**

> *"The stress test found that **no protocol exists for supporting a member who acted in good faith and faces legal consequence** **[F-10]**. **Abandoning such a member is the single most damaging possible internal event.**"* **[S-A:729]**

| Stage | **Action** | **Owner** | **Time** | **Current executability** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **0 Immediate** | **Member contacts the published safety/legal number; the organisation acknowledges and tells the member what to do and what not to do — do not sign anything, do not give a statement without advice where advice is available, preserve the record** | **Safety Lead + National Legal** | **Same hour** | **`NOT EXECUTABLE` — no published number, no Safety Lead appointment, no National Legal** |
| **1 Triage** | **National Legal determines: is this within doctrine? Is cover available? Is external counsel required? Is the organisation a party or a witness?** | **National Legal **[S: Ch.5]**** | **Same day** | **`NOT EXECUTABLE` — and "is cover available" cannot be answered: **L1 … L15 = 0 of 15**** |
| **2 Support decision** | **Where the member acted within doctrine and in good faith, the organisation provides support as determined by counsel and by the approval matrix **[S: App. E]**, and says so in writing** | **National Legal + CEO** | **Within a defined short period** | **`NOT EXECUTABLE` — no counsel instructed, no approval matrix, **CEA `GF-1`: `APPROVES` unassigned in 13 of 16 acts**** |
| **3 Where conduct is in question** | **Support for the person's basic rights while the matter is reviewed under **[S: SOP-18]**; support does not imply endorsement; withdrawal is a National Legal decision recorded with reasons** | **National Legal + Audit & Ethics** | **Per review** | **`NOT EXECUTABLE`** |
| **4 Records** | **Everything preserved — incident record, training certificates, authorisation records, supervision records, communications; legal hold applied; retention suspended for the affected records (**A-17**)** | **AI & Data + Legal** | **Immediately** | **`PARTIALLY EXECUTABLE` — **this is RSK-13's control, it needs no entity, and it is the only stage that can begin today on paper** (CEA §XXI.1, `ID-13`)** |
| **5 Notification** | **Insurer notified per **L14**; authorities as required; Council informed where the matter is serious or public** | **National Legal** | **Per obligations** | **`NOT EXECUTABLE` — **no insurer exists to notify**, no Council exists to inform** |
| **6 Communication** | **Single authorised spokesperson **[S: Ch.24]**; no local comment; no member named publicly without legal advice and, where applicable, the person's consent** | **Communications + Legal** | **As required** | **`NOT EXECUTABLE` — and **`A-07`** may not be published at all while §9.2 rule 2 is in force** |
| **7 After** | **Institutional review, lessons-register entry (**A-15**), protocol/training correction, member welfare check** | **Safety + HR** | **Within a defined period** | **`NOT EXECUTABLE`** |

> ### **`EIGHT STAGES. ONE IS PARTIALLY EXECUTABLE TODAY. SEVEN ARE NOT. AND THE PROTOCOL ITSELF IS THE CORPUS'S EVIDENCE THAT `F-10` IS ALREADY OPEN.`**

# PART XVII — THE FOUR UNPAID CONTROL ROLES

## XVII.1 The finding this Part exists to put in front of a professional

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **§XII.2 `FM-1`, `FM-2`**, funding-map rows 6, 7, 9 and 10; **`PC-07`**, **`COI-1`**, **`COI-2`**, **`NM-3`**, **`NM-10`**; mandate §19.

> ### **`THE ARCHITECTURE PROHIBITS FOUR CONTROL ROLES FROM EARNING, AND DOES NOT FUND THEM.`**
>
> *"**`FM-1`** — 8 of 14 rows have no funding source in practice. This is not a percentage problem; it is a source problem."*
> *"**`FM-2`** — The 4 roles that may not participate in paid ventures at all — **Funds Manager, HR & Verification, Safety/Protection, Volunteer Manager** — are precisely the roles with no funding source. **The architecture prohibits them from earning and does not fund them.**"* **[CEA: §XII.2, *the map's findings*, lines 1061–1066]**
>
> ### **`AND UNPAID IS NOT THE SAME AS UNEXPOSED.`**
>
> **Mandate §19. **This Part states the finding and the questions. It does not state that any role-holder is personally liable, and it does not state that any role-holder is protected. Both statements are prohibited — mandate §8, §18 — and both would be conclusions.**

| Role | **Short form** | **What it controls** | **The prohibition that makes it unpaid** | **The funding source the architecture names** | **Actual state** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Funds Manager** | **`FM`** | **Every money movement; custody of funds; operation of the mandate; the record of every payment** | **`COI-1`** — *"segregated: may not participate in any venture"* **[CEA §XII.2 row 7]**; **`COI-1 … COI-4`**; the four-hand rule **[appendix.txt:6925–6948]** | **Priority 5** | **`NONE IN PRACTICE` · `PC-07`** |
| **HR & Verification Manager** | **`HRV`** | **Verification of work and of members — the entire population that **`CD-06`**'s *"verified work"* depends on; the anti-gaming tests; the leaderboard's inputs** | **`COI-2`** — *"it may not be paid from what it verifies"* **[CEA §XII.2 row 6]**; **`PC-07`** | **Priority 5** | **`NONE IN PRACTICE` · `PC-07`** |
| **Safety / Protection Lead** | **`SPL`** | **The protection pillar; incident response; the **§9.2** activation gate; **§9.3** stages 0 and 7; **`A-20`** sealed-category integrity matters** | **`NM-3`** — *"prohibits participation in any revenue activity"* **[CEA §XII.2 row 9]**; **CEA `J-15`** — `NM-1 … NM-12` a permanent rejection | **Priority 5 *"in principle"*** | **`NONE IN PRACTICE`** — *"an unfunded mandatory protection function — the worst combination in the map"* |
| **Volunteer Manager** | **`VM`** | **The member lifecycle; engagement, retention, exit and stay interviews; consent; the population every mission activity depends on** | **`NM-10`** **[CEA §XII.2 row 10]**; **CEA `J-15`** | **Priority 5 *"in principle"*** | **`NONE IN PRACTICE` — *"same finding"*** |

| **Internal Auditor** | **CEA **§XII.2 row 11** — class `MISSION-ONLY / CONTROL`, funding `NONE IN PRACTICE`, verdict *"independence is the requirement; funding is the threat to it — `COI-2`"*; **`AU-1 … AU-12`**, **`AF-2`**, **`AU-11` `EXTERNAL ONLY`**; **[S-A:2343 V7]**, **[S-B:4002 EV-8]** | **Mandate §19 names four unpaid control roles, and CEA **`FM-2`** names the same four. **The Internal Auditor is a fifth: equally unpaid, equally prohibited from revenue participation, and the only role whose entire value is independence. **It is examined at **UD-12** below and at CEA **§XXII.2**, where **`AU-11`** concludes that internal independence is unachievable for founder compensation with eleven people. **Nothing is added to `FM-2`; the count remains four**** |

## XVII.4 The count

| Finding | **Result** |
|---|---|
| **Roles examined** | **4** *(a fifth, the Internal Auditor, is recorded at §XVII.2 and not added to `FM-2`)* |
| **Dimensions per role** | **14** |
| **Cells in the matrix** | **4 × 14 = 56** |
| **Cells that are settled** | **8 — `UD-01` and `UD-02` for all four roles. **Both are settled **against** the role-holder: the role is mandatory and it may not be paid**** |
| **Cells that are `PROFESSIONAL QUESTION`** | **8 — `UD-06` and `UD-07` for all four roles. **These are the liability and fiduciary questions, and **none is answered in this brief**** |
| **Cells that are `UNKNOWN`** | **12 — `UD-04`, `UD-05`, `UD-08` for all four roles** |
| **Cells that are `NOT SATISFIABLE TODAY`** | **4 — `UD-10` for all four roles** |
| **Cells that are `NO POPULATION`** | **4 — `UD-12` for all four roles** |
| **Cells that are `REQUIRES GOVERNANCE DECISION`** | **4 — `UD-13` for all four roles** |
| **Cells that are `SPECIFIED · NOT EXECUTED`** | **4 — `UD-14` for all four roles. **This is the only dimension where work can begin today**** |
| **Cells that are `NONE IN PRACTICE`** | **4 — `UD-03` for all four roles** |
| **Cells that are `FAILS`** | **4 — `UD-09` for all four roles** |
| **Sum** | **8 + 8 + 12 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 4 = 56 · `EXACTLY 56, ONE STATE PER CELL`** |

> ### **`FIFTY-SIX CELLS. EIGHT SETTLED, AND EVERY ONE OF THE EIGHT IS SETTLED AGAINST THE ROLE-HOLDER. TWELVE UNKNOWN. EIGHT PROFESSIONAL QUESTIONS. THE REMAINING TWENTY-EIGHT ARE BLOCKED, UNSATISFIABLE, UNPOPULATED, UNDECIDED OR MERELY SPECIFIED.`**

## XVII.5 What this Part refuses to say

| Refusal | **Ground** |
|---|---|
| **No statement that any of the four *"is personally liable"*** | **Mandate §8, §18. **`UD-06` is a `PROFESSIONAL QUESTION` in every one of the four columns, and **L6**, **L7**, **RSK-6**, **RSK-7** are all `UNKNOWN`** |
| **No statement that any of the four *"is protected"*** | **Same. **L9** asks what protections apply *"if any"*; **§9.2 rule 4** bars any representation of cover; **`PP-06`** |
| **No statement that unpaid status removes, reduces or increases liability** | **Whether liability attaches to a **function** or to **remuneration** is exactly the question **`UD-06`** and **`UD-07`** put to counsel. **Answering it here would be answering it**** |
| **No statement that any role-holder is a volunteer, an employee, a worker, a contractor, an officer, a trustee or a fiduciary** | **`UD-05`, **`UD-07**; **`SR-02`, **`PP-05`, **`PP-07`, **CD-01**, **CD-18** are all `UNKNOWN`** |
| **No proposal to pay any of the four from any source** | **`UD-02` is `PROHIBITED` for all four; **`J-15`** is permanent; **`GD-02`** is a governance decision and not this brief's to make. **The delta audit records the honest statement of the problem: *"the organisation may not pay"* — `A-N16` remains blocked and **`PP-04`** applies**** |
| **No proposal that the founder pay any of the four personally** | **`REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` + `REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`** **[S-K(E): §XXVI]**; and it would engage **`SEP-5`**, **`AG-10`**, **`AH-15`**, **`TX-16`** |
| **No number — no stipend, honorarium, allowance, reimbursement, premium or cost per member** | **Mandate §24; **`L2`** asks *"at what cost per member"* and the answer is `UNKNOWN`; **V-01 … V-22** remain empty** |
| **No statement that the roles may be abolished** | **`UD-01` is `SOURCE REQUIREMENT — MANDATORY` for all four. **CEA `F-10`** permits funding, formal suspension or formal transfer — **all three are governance decisions with a published choice**, and abolition is not among them** |

# PART XVIII — THE ECONOMIC AUTHORITY GAP REGISTER

## XVIII.1 The twelve fields, and the one rule that governs all of them

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **§XX.1**, **§XX.2**, **§XX.3 `GF-1 … GF-6`**; mandate §20. **The CEA's matrix of sixteen economic acts against eight governance functions is preserved without amendment. This Part adds four fields to each act, which converts a status table into a work package.**

| Field | **Name** | **Source** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **`ACT`** — the economic act, exactly as the CEA names it | **CEA §XX.2** |
| **2** | **`PROPOSES`** | **CEA §XX.1 function 1** |
| **3** | **`APPROVES`** | **CEA §XX.1 function 2 — *"not the proposer, and never the beneficiary"*; combination `NO — ABSOLUTE`** |
| **4** | **`SIGNS`** | **CEA §XX.1 function 3 — *"today no one can sign, because no legal person exists (`SR-01`, `A-N16`)"*** |
| **5** | **`RECEIVES`** | **CEA §XX.1 function 4 — combination `NO — COI-1`** |
| **6** | **`AUTHORISES`** | **CEA §XX.1 function 5 — *"never the same hands as 4"*; combination `NO — ABSOLUTE`** |
| **7** | **`VERIFIES`** | **CEA §XX.1 function 6 — *"not paid from what it verifies"*; combination `NO — COI-2, EL-2`** |
| **8** | **`CALCULATES`** | **CEA §XX.1 function 7** |
| **9** | **`AUDITS`** | **CEA §XX.1 function 8 — *"detects; never cures, clears, classifies or waives"*; combination `NO — COI-2`** |
| **10** | **`EXECUTABLE TODAY?`** — **added by this Part** | **CEA `GF-2`: *"sixteen acts, zero executable"*** |
| **11** | **`THE BLOCKING ABSENCE, NAMED`** — **added by this Part** | **The identifiers that make field 10 read `NO`** |
| **12** | **`THE DECISION THAT WOULD FILL IT`** — **added by this Part** | **`GD-xx`, `SR-xx`, `CQ-xx`, `CD-xx`, `TX-xx`, `AH-xx` — **a decision, never a person**** |

> ### **`THE RULE: A VACANCY IS FILLED BY A DECISION, NOT BY A NAME.`**
>
> **Mandate §20: *"never fill a vacancy with an invented person or body."* CEA **`GF-6`**: *"nothing in this matrix is resolved by this instrument. Every `UNASSIGNED` is carried to §XX.4 as an open decision. **`EA-24`** prohibits silent resolution."* **`DA-30`**: authority is not invented, and is not conferred by disclosure or by repetition.**
>
> **Every cell in fields 2 to 9 below is either a **role the CEA already names**, or the CEA's own word — **`UNASSIGNED`**, **`BLOCKED`**, **`AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`**, **`Not applicable`**. **No cell in this Part contains a person, a name, a body, a committee, a council or an office that the corpus does not already name. Where the corpus names a body that does not exist — *"National"*, *"Team Leader"*, *"Council"* — the cell says so and field 11 records the absence.**

## XVIII.3 The six findings, preserved

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **§XX.3 `GF-1 … GF-6`**. **Reproduced without amendment, with the arithmetic of `GF-1` made explicit because mandate §20 requires the counts to be preserved exactly.**

| ID | **The CEA's finding — unchanged** | **This Part's check** |
|---|---|---|
| **`GF-1`** | ***"The `APPROVES` column is `UNASSIGNED` in thirteen of sixteen acts.** The architecture has proposers, verifiers and auditors, and almost no approvers. **This is not a drafting gap; it is the absence of a governance layer, and it is the single largest obstacle to any economic activity whatsoever."*** | **`CONFIRMED — 13 OF 16.`** Thirteen = every act whose `APPROVES` cell is `UNASSIGNED`, `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`, `Not applicable`, or names a body that does not exist. **The three exceptions are acts 3, 4 and 14** — and act 3's approver holds *"within delegated authority"* for which **no delegation instrument exists** (**`LDR-U19`**, **`A-N17`**), while act 14's approver is the **Funds Manager**, whom **`COI-1`** segregates from venture participation. **The thirteen are acts 1, 2, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 15 and 16** |
| **`GF-2`** | ***"The `SIGNS` column is `BLOCKED` in every act that involves money. `SR-01` and `A-N16` together mean that no economic act in this architecture can currently be executed. **Sixteen acts, zero executable."*** | **`CONFIRMED — 0 OF 16.`** Field 10 reads `NO` in all sixteen rows. **`SIGNS` is `BLOCKED` in eleven acts — 3, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 15, 16 — and `Not applicable` in the five that involve no external instrument** |
| **`GF-3`** | ***"The `CALCULATES` column is empty for compensation, because the formula does not exist.** Everything downstream of it — payment sheets, accruals, version control, T1 — is therefore also empty."*** | **`CONFIRMED.`** Act 5's field 8 is `UNASSIGNED`, and act 6's reads *"as above"*. **`CD-08`, `CD-09`, `SR-15`, `V-01 … V-03`** |
| **`GF-4`** | ***"`VERIFIES` is the only column that is substantially filled, and it is filled by one role — HR & Verification — which is also the role that cannot be paid from what it verifies.** One verifier across sixteen acts is a single point of failure and a segregation failure simultaneously."*** | **`CONFIRMED`** — and it is **`UD-10`** for **`HRV`**: `NOT SATISFIABLE TODAY`, because eleven people cannot supply four independent hands (**CEA `F-11`**, **`GD-11`**)** |
| **`GF-5`** | ***"Founder compensation cannot be governed internally at all.** Proposer: nobody may. Approver: recused. Verifier: conflicted. **The only available auditor is external — which requires money, which returns to `H-Q17` and to `C_L1`."*** | **`CONFIRMED` — and note **ER-1**: the CEA's reference to **`H-Q17`** here should be **`GD-02`**. **The corpus's `H-Q17` is *"Can Sewak Sathi pay Krytos?"* and is registered at **CQ-31**. **The circularity `GF-5` identifies is real either way: an external audit requires money, and money requires the authority that the external audit is needed to establish**** |
| **`GF-6`** | ***"Nothing in this matrix is resolved by this instrument.** Every `UNASSIGNED` is carried to §XX.4 as an open decision. **`EA-24` prohibits silent resolution."*** | **`CONFIRMED AND EXTENDED.`** This Part resolves nothing either. **Every `UNASSIGNED` is carried to field 12 as a decision identifier, to §XVIII.4 as a routing, and to Part XIX as a professional question. **No vacancy is filled**** |

## XVIII.4 Routing — who must decide, who must advise, and what it depends on

**[P]** Three fields this Part adds to each act, so that a single instruction can be issued per discipline — the method the corpus itself used at **[S-A: Annexure B]**, **[S-B: Annexure C]** and **[S-C: Annexure C]**.

| Act | **Who decides** | **Who advises** | **Entity dependency** | **Depends on** | **Register ref.** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1 Approve a venture** | **Governance — `GD-01`, a founder decision** | **Corporate counsel, commercial counsel** | **NO for the design · YES for the approval's effect** | **`GD-01`, `SR-98`** | **`CQ-10`** |
| **2 Set a price** | **Governance — `GD-01`** | **Chartered accountant, commercial counsel, tax counsel** | **NO for the design · YES for the supply** | **`SR-22`, `SR-16`, `B-11`** | **`CQ-16`** |
| **3 Accept a customer** | **The entity, once it exists** | **Corporate counsel, banking adviser, commercial counsel** | **`YES`** | **`SR-01`, `SR-03`, `CQ-12`** | **`CQ-12`** |
| **4 Record verified work** | **Governance — `GD-01` appointments** | **Chartered accountant, records specialist, internal control design** | **`NO` — the record can be built today** | **`AH-16`, `CD-21`, `ID-13`** | **`CQ-21`** |
| **5 Calculate compensation** | **Governance — the formula's approval** | **Chartered accountant, labour counsel, tax counsel** | **NO for the design · YES for any payment** | **`CD-08`, `SR-15`, `SR-02`** | **`CQ-09`** |
| **6 Pay compensation** | **The entity and the approval authority** | **Labour counsel, tax counsel, chartered accountant, banking adviser** | **`YES`** | **`SR-01` → `SR-02` → `SR-03` → `GD-01` → `CD-08`** | **`CQ-05 … CQ-09`** |
| **7 Pay a statutory obligation** | **The entity** | **Tax counsel, chartered accountant, labour counsel** | **`YES`** | **`TX-11`, `CD-12`, `CD-13`** | **`CQ-16`, `CQ-17`** |
| **8 Draw a reserve** | **Governance — `GD-03`** | **Chartered accountant, banking adviser, corporate counsel** | **`YES`** | **`SR-19` with `SR-03`, `V-07`, `GD-09`** | **`CQ-20`** |
| **9 Draw the float `P-6`** | **Governance — `GD-02`, and `P-6`'s approval** | **Chartered accountant, tax counsel, banking adviser** | **`YES`** | **`GD-02`, `HC-3`, `AH-06`** | **`CQ-19`, `CQ-20`** |
| **10 Accept a contribution** | **Governance — `GD-01`, with `AG-10` disclosure** | **Governance counsel, chartered accountant, tax counsel** | **`YES`** | **`SEP-5`, `AH-15`, `D-3`** | **`CQ-15`** |
| **11 Approve founder compensation** | **Nobody today — `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED`** | **Governance counsel, tax counsel, an external auditor** | **`YES`** | **`GD-08`, `SR-20`, `CF-16`, `AU-11`** | **`CQ-11`, `CQ-30`, `CQ-32`** |
| **12 Authorise a related-party transaction** | **A disinterested approver who does not exist** | **Corporate counsel, tax counsel, IP counsel, valuer** | **`YES`** | **`SR-12`, `SR-13`, `FC-04`, `RP-5`** | **`CQ-33`, `CQ-35`** |
| **13 Spend on mission activity** | **Governance — `GD-13`, and `MI-1`'s approval** | **Chartered accountant, tax counsel, charitable-regulation counsel** | **`YES`** | **`GD-02`, `HC-13`, `S-6`, `BP-1 … BP-5`** | **`CQ-17`, `CQ-19`** |
| **14 Classify an expense** | **A human — AI may flag only** | **Chartered accountant, internal control design** | **`NO` for the rule · YES for the payment** | **`AG-02`, `AH-09`, `GD-07 RI-3`** | **`CQ-20`** |
| **15 Close a venture** | **Governance — `GD-04`** | **Corporate counsel, chartered accountant, labour counsel** | **`YES`** | **`HG-5`, `LC-2`, `HC-14`** | **`CQ-10`** |
| **16 Amend the waterfall** | **A Tier-1 amendment — *"National + professional validation + the founder's formal amendment"*** | **Tax counsel, chartered accountant, corporate counsel, governance counsel** | **`YES`** | **`GC-01` first, then `GD-07`, `GD-08`; `DA-31` bars any successor instrument** | **`CQ-17`, `CQ-18`, `CQ-30`** |

## XVIII.5 The register counted

| Count | **Result** |
|---|---|
| **Acts** | **16 — unchanged from CEA §XX.2** |
| **Fields per act** | **12 — nine preserved, three added** |
| **Cells in the register** | **16 × 12 = 192** |
| **Acts executable today** | **`0 OF 16`** — **`GF-2` preserved exactly** |
| **Acts whose `APPROVES` cell is unassigned, unresolved, inapplicable or names a non-existent body** | **`13 OF 16`** — **`GF-1` preserved exactly; the three exceptions are acts 3, 4 and 14** |
| **Acts whose `SIGNS` cell is `BLOCKED`** | **11** — and **`GF-2`** is satisfied because every act that involves money is among them |

| **Acts whose design can begin today without an entity** | **5 — acts 1, 2, 4, 5, 14** · and of those, **only act 4's record and act 14's attribution rule are executable today** |
| **Acts requiring a Tier-1 amendment** | **1 — act 16** |
| **Persons, names, bodies or offices invented in this Part** | **`0`** |
| **Vacancies filled in this Part** | **`0`** |

# PART XIX — THE PROFESSIONAL QUESTION REGISTER `CQ-01 … CQ-35`

## XIX.1 The fifteen fields, the rule against manufactured answers, and the block index

**[P]** Mandate §21: *"a register of professional questions, fifteen fields each, with no manufactured answers."* **Thirty-five questions are registered in seven blocks. Every question carries all fifteen fields. Fields 11 and 12 are identical in every row and are identical by design: they are not this brief's to complete.**

| # | **Field** | **Rule** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **`THE QUESTION`** | **Framed so that it can be answered in writing by a named professional. **Never framed so that the desired answer is implied** — mandate §28: the pack *"must not tell counsel what to answer"*** |
| **2** | **`PROFESSIONAL DISCIPLINE`** | **The discipline that holds the answer, named as the corpus names it — **`[S-A: Annexure B]`**, **`[S-B: Annexure C]`**, **`[S-C: Annexure C]`** |
| **3** | **`ORIGIN`** | **The identifier that raises the question. **A question with no origin is not registered**** |
| **4** | **`CURRENT ARCHITECTURAL STATUS`** | **The `[SOURCE]` state, unchanged** |
| **5** | **`CLASS`** | **One of the ten classes at §V.3 — the primary class** |
| **6** | **`SECONDARY CLASS`** | **Any further class that applies, so that no question is routed to only one professional** |
| **7** | **`FACTS REQUIRED`** | **What only the founder and the role-holders can supply** |
| **8** | **`DOCUMENTS REQUIRED`** | **What must be in counsel's hands** |
| **9** | **`ENTITY DEPENDENCY`** | **`YES` · `NO` · `PARTLY` — stated, never assumed** |
| **10** | **`TAGS`** | **From the seven tags at §IV. **Never upgraded: an `ASSERTION` does not become a `FACT`, a `PROPOSAL` does not become a `DECISION`, a `QUESTION` does not become an `ANSWER**** |
| **11** | **`PROFESSIONAL DETERMINATION`** | **`[TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL]` — in every row, without exception** |
| **12** | **`CONDITIONS / ASSUMPTIONS`** | **`[TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL]` — in every row, without exception** |
| **13** | **`CONSEQUENCE IF ANSWERED`** | **What unblocks** |
| **14** | **`CONSEQUENCE IF NOT ANSWERED`** | **What remains blocked** |
| **15** | **`NEXT DEPENDENCY`** | **The identifier that this question's answer feeds** |

> ### **`NO ANSWER IS MANUFACTURED ANYWHERE IN THIS PART. FIELDS 11 AND 12 READ `[TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL]` IN ALL THIRTY-FIVE ROWS, AND ANY ROW IN WHICH THEY READ OTHERWISE IS A DEFECT.`**

| Block | **Questions** | **Subject** | **The corpus register it reconciles with** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **A** | **`CQ-01 … CQ-04`** | **The constitutive document set** | **[S-A:2337 V1]** · **[S-B:3998 EV-4]** · **[S-C:4127 HV-6]** · **`SR-01`** |
| **B** | **`CQ-05 … CQ-09`** | **Worker classification and its consequences** | **[S-A:2339 V3]** · **[S-B:3997 EV-3]** · **[S-C:4122 HV-1]** · **`SR-02`** |

| **D** | **`CQ-16 … CQ-21`** | **Tax, accounting, records and retention** | **[S-A:2338 V2]** · **[S-A:2348 V12]** · **[S-B:3995 EV-1]** · **[S-B:3996 EV-2]** · **[S-C:4130 HV-9]** |

# PART XX — PROVENANCE AND TRACEABILITY

## XX.1 The rule, and the six-link chain

**[P]** Mandate §23. **Every identifier used in this brief is traceable through six links. An identifier that cannot be traced through all six is not a citation; it is a rumour.**

| Link | **What it records** | **Failure mode it prevents** |
|---|---|---|
| **1** | **`CURRENT ID`** — the identifier as this brief uses it | Silent renaming, which is how **ER-1**, **ER-2** and **ER-5** arise |
| **2** | **`INSTRUMENT`** — the file that holds it, by its short form | Attribution to a document that does not contain it |
| **3** | **`SECTION / TABLE / ROW`** — the exact location, by line number where the instrument is a plain-text file | *"Somewhere in v1.3-A"*, which is not a location |
| **4** | **`THE QUESTION IT CARRIES`** — what is actually being asked | Answering a question the corpus never asked |
| **5** | **`THE EVIDENCE REQUIRED`** — what would close it, in the corpus's own words where it states them | Treating a discussion as a decision |
| **6** | **`DEPENDENCY`** — what must be true first | Commissioning work that cannot be performed |

## XX.6 What traceability does not buy

| Limit | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **A location is not an answer** | **Every identifier above is located. **Not one of them is answered.** Locating `SR-01` at **[S-F:1878]** establishes that the question exists, that it is Tier 0, that its evidence is *"a written decision plus counsel opinion"*, and that its next action has been performed six times without effect. **It does not establish what the answer is** |
| **A citation is not a document** | **Rule v1.3-J: **`NO SOURCE → NO FACT → NO CLOSURE`**. **A reference to a document is not the document. **`SR-21` is located; the blueprint is not present**** |
| **An assertion is not a fact** | **Rule K-4. **`FA-1 … FA-7` are located, quoted and tagged — and remain assertions** |
| **A repeated statement is not a decision** | **Rule **`DA-30`**. **The two-signatory rule appears at four separate locations in **[S-A]** alone — 879, 1032, 1642 and 2346 — and is still not a mandate: **`I-PD-5`, [S-K(E)]**** |
| **A trace is not authority** | **This brief is **`NOT LEGAL ADVICE`**. **Its traceability makes it auditable, which is not the same as making it right** |

# PART XXI — DECISION DEPENDENCIES

## XXI.1 What a dependency is here, and the six kinds

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — CEA **Part XXXI.1 `ID-1 … ID-18`**; **CEA §XXIX.4**; **[S-F:162 `CP-2`]**; **[S-F:1878–1975]**. **A dependency in this brief is not a task. It is a fact, a document, a person or a professional determination that must exist before a question can be answered at all.** A task can be scheduled; a dependency cannot be skipped, and no amount of drafting removes one.

| # | **Kind** | **Definition** | **How it is removed** | **How it must not be removed** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **`ENTITY-DEPENDENT`** | The question has no answer until a legal person exists to be the subject of it | **`SR-01` is decided and a constitutive document is created** | **By treating an assertion as a legal person — **`FA-1 … FA-7`, `PP-01 … PP-09`, Rule K-4** |
| **2** | **`DOCUMENT-DEPENDENT`** | The question's answer lives in a document that has not been produced | **The document is received and verified — **REQUEST → RECEIVE → VERIFY → VALIDATE → DECIDE → UPDATE** | **By describing the document, summarising it, or citing the reference to it. **A reference to a document is not the document**** |
| **3** | **`PROFESSION-DEPENDENT`** | The question can only be answered by a qualified professional, and no such answer exists | **A written professional determination, in counsel's own words, with conditions and assumptions** | **By this brief answering it. **Fields 11 and 12 read `[TO BE COMPLETED BY COUNSEL]` in all 35 rows and are not this brief's to fill**** |
| **4** | **`FACT-DEPENDENT`** | The question needs a fact that only the founder or a role-holder holds | **The founder supplies the fact in the disclosure pack, **Part XXIV** | **By inference from what the founder has said elsewhere. **`FA-R2`: a declaration is not a fact until verified**** |
| **5** | **`HUMAN-DECISION-DEPENDENT`** | The question is answerable but requires a human to choose, and a choice that carries a conflict requires a disinterested chooser | **A recorded decision by a person or body with authority** | **By an instrument selecting the entity — mandate §10. **This brief does not select, recommend or rank**** |
| **6** | **`MEASUREMENT-DEPENDENT`** | The question cannot be answered before operation produces data | **Operating, and measuring** | **By estimating. **`SR-15`'s own words: *"pilot measures it"*, *"do not decide before measurement"*** |

## XXI.3 `CP-2` — the critical path, verbatim, and what each link in it requires

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — **[S-F:162]**, the corpus's own consolidated critical path, answering *"What is the critical path?"* with ***"`CP-2` LEGAL / COMPLIANCE, and it is not close."*** **It is reproduced here unchanged. This brief adds no link, removes no link, and does not shorten it. The instrument adds: *"eight paths are mapped in Part VII; `CP-2` has the greatest downstream blocking effect because seven of the eight other paths contain at least one item that cannot lawfully operate without an entity."***

> ### **`"ENTITY STRUCTURE DECIDED → COUNSEL VALIDATES TAX, EMPLOYMENT AND CLASSIFICATION → INSURANCE ANSWERS OBTAINED → BANK MANDATE AND TWO SIGNATORIES ESTABLISHED → WORKER COMPENSATION CAN LAWFULLY BE PAID → THE PILOT CAN PAY ANYONE → ANYTHING ECONOMIC CAN BE MEASURED."`** — **[S-F:162, `CP-2`]**

| Link | **What it is** | **The professional discipline that answers it** | **The questions in this brief** | **Position today** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Entity structure decided** | **Corporate / entity counsel** | **`CQ-01 … CQ-04`, `CQ-28`** | **`NOT DONE`. **`SR-01` `DECISION REQUIRED`, `BLOCKER A / Tier 0`; next action *"Commission counsel"* performed six times without advancing the register — **U-PD-16**, and a seventh time by this brief**** |
| **2** | **Counsel validates tax, employment and classification** | **Tax counsel; labour counsel** | **`CQ-05 … CQ-09`, `CQ-16 … CQ-18`, `CQ-33`** | **`NOT REACHED`** — link 1 stands before it |
| **3** | **Insurance answers obtained** | **Insurance broker; liability counsel** | **`CQ-22 … CQ-24`** | **`NOT REACHED`. **§9.2 rule 1: no written confirmation, no activation**** |
| **4** | **Bank mandate and two signatories established** | **Banking counsel; the entity's own officers** | **`CQ-10`, **Part X**, **Part XVIII**** | **`NOT REACHED`. **`T-1 … T-5` = 0 of 5; **`BLK-A-03`; **`PP-04`**** |
| **5** | **Worker compensation can lawfully be paid** | **Labour counsel; tax counsel** | **`CQ-05 … CQ-09`, `CQ-33`** | **`NOT REACHED`. **`C-15` and `C-16`: any external payment, and priority-1 worker compensation, are both prohibited until this link closes**** |
| **6** | **The pilot can pay anyone** | **Governance counsel; the founder** | **`CQ-13`, **Part XXVII**** | **`NOT REACHED`. **`C-25`: running the pilot is prohibited**** |
| **7** | **Anything economic can be measured** | **The accounting profession** | **`CQ-20`, `CQ-21`, **Part XV**** | **`NOT REACHED`. **No measurement is possible before a payment exists to measure — and **`SR-15`** cannot be decided before measurement**** |

> ### **`THE CRITICAL PATH IS SEVEN LINKS LONG, EVERY LINK IS BLOCKED BY THE ONE BEFORE IT, AND THE FIRST LINK IS NOT AN ANALYTICAL TASK. IT IS A COMMISSION. NO PART OF THIS BRIEF IS THE FIRST LINK.`**

**[P]** Computed from the register: **field 9, `ENTITY DEPENDENCY`, across all 35 rows — 21 `YES`, 7 `PARTLY`, 6 qualified `NO`, 1 unqualified `NO`.** **Seven questions therefore carry an element that does not wait for `SR-01`. They are listed here so that nothing in this brief implies the whole register is idle.**

| ID | **Field 9 as written in the register** | **The part that needs no entity** | **The part that does** | **Primary class** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`CQ-06`** | **`NO`** — the only unqualified `NO` in the register | **The whole question: what happened, when, to whom. It is a question of historical fact, and facts do not wait for a legal person** | **Nothing. **Its answer then becomes evidence for **`CQ-05`** and **`SR-02`**** | **⑦ Requires factual evidence** |
| **`CQ-21`** | **`NO` for the design and the paper record · `YES` for the retention period, which is a legal question** | **Designing the record and its fields, and beginning a paper record today** | **The retention period, which counsel must set** | **① Answerable now** |
| **`CQ-25`** | **`NO` for the boundary's statement · `YES` for the organisation's own exposure** | **Stating the criminal boundary that already governs every person involved** | **The organisation's exposure, which needs a legal person** | **⑥ Insurance and liability** |
| **`CQ-26`** | **`NO` for the duties' statement · `YES` for the organisation's own registration obligations** | **Stating the safeguarding duties that already bind individuals** | **Registration obligations, which attach to an entity** | **⑥ Insurance and liability** |
| **`CQ-27`** | **`NO` for the record design · `YES` for fiduciary status, which requires a legal person** | **Designing the data-handling record** | **Fiduciary status, and therefore `SR-05` and `SR-06`** | **⑥ Insurance and liability** |
| **`CQ-28`** | **`NO` for the search · `YES` for the registration** | **Searching name availability** | **Registering a name, which requires an applicant** | **② Requires entity selection** |
| **`CQ-33`** | **`NO` for the rule's design · `YES` for any payment computed under it** | **Designing the withholding or deduction rule** | **Any payment, which requires **`ID-4`**** | **④ Tax and accounting** |

> ### **`SEVEN OF THIRTY-FIVE. IN EVERY CASE BUT ONE THE ENTITY-INDEPENDENT PART IS A DESIGN, A STATEMENT OR A SEARCH — NOT A DETERMINATION. NOTHING ON THIS LIST PRODUCES AN ANSWER THAT A PROFESSIONAL MUST GIVE. IT PRODUCES WORK THAT CAN BE DONE WHILE THE PROFESSIONAL WORK IS COMMISSIONED.`**

## XXI.5 Circular dependencies — three, and none is resolvable by analysis

**[P]** `[SOURCE]`. **A circular dependency is one where the body that would decide is itself the subject of the decision, or where the evidence required can only be produced by the act the evidence is supposed to authorise. These are recorded, not solved. Solving one requires a human act outside the circle.**

| # | **The circle** | **Its two arcs** | **Corpus anchor** | **What breaks it** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **Succession** | **An apex succession mechanism can only be adopted by a body with authority to adopt it · the body with authority to adopt it is the one whose succession is in question** | **`LDR-U19` ***"not named anywhere in the source hierarchy"*** **[S-D:4594]**; **[S-F:159]**: three decisions have `AUTHORITY UNRESOLVED` — `LDR-U01`, `A-N19`, `LDR-U19` — ***"in each case the body that would decide is itself the subject of the decision"*** | **The founder, acting personally, or a court, or a professional appointed for the purpose. **`REQUIRES COUNSEL`** — and the counsel engagement must itself be signed and paid for by someone, which is Part XXIII's whole problem** |
| **2** | **Payment for the advice** | **Professional advice is required before any payment can lawfully be made · the advice itself must be paid for** | **`C-15`** — *"any external payment"* is prohibited **[S-K(E):1599]**, quoted at **[S-I: §XIII.3]** and preserved verbatim at **[S-K(E):1139]**: ***"no external payment can lawfully or operationally be made"***; **`A-N16 REMAINS BLOCKED`; `PP-04`; **`BLK-D-07`** — the professional fees for `BLK-B-01 … BLK-B-11`, whose variable is *"not stated anywhere"* **[S-F:695]**** | **The founder paying personally — which is `REQUIRES FOUNDER DECISION` and `REQUIRES QUALIFIED TAX ADVICE`, and which this brief neither decides nor recommends **[S-K(E): §XXVI]** |
| **3** | **Classification and operation** | **Worker classification requires the facts of how people actually work · the facts of how people work cannot be produced without operating · operating requires the pilot, which is prohibited** | **`C-25`** — running the pilot is prohibited **[S-K(E):1609]**; **`SR-15`** — *"pilot measures it"*, *"do not decide before measurement"* **[S-F:1892]**; **`SR-02`** depends on `SR-01` | **`CQ-06` is the escape hatch the register already contains: the historical facts of who did what, when, and for whom can be supplied without operating anything. **That is why `CQ-06` carries the only unqualified `NO` in field 9** |

## XXI.6 False dependencies — seven things that look blocked and are not

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` and `[PROFESSIONAL QUESTION]`. **A false dependency is a belief that a question cannot be answered until something else happens, where the something else is not actually required by the question. Each is recorded so that it is not used, later, as a reason to do nothing.**

| # | **The apparent dependency** | **Why it is false** | **The evidence that it is false** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | ***"`PVR-03` cannot be answered until the entity is chosen"*** | **Classification principles are a question of law applied to facts. The facts — who works, how, under whose direction, for what — exist now and are the same whatever form is chosen** | **The CEA's own finding: **`PVR-03`, `PVR-05` and `PVR-22` are answerable before an entity exists** — **[CEA §XXIX.4]**; and mandate §7 requires the entity/framework dependency to be *stated*, not waited for |
| **2** | ***"`PVR-05` cannot be answered until an accounting framework is adopted"*** | **The four meanings of revenue — **`REV-1 … REV-4`** — are conceptually distinct whether or not a framework is chosen. What cannot be done is *recognise* revenue; what can be done is state what would have to be recognised** | **Part VII separates **ACCOUNTING REVENUE**, **CASH RECEIVED**, **AVAILABLE DISTRIBUTABLE-OPERATING CASH** and **INTERNAL ECONOMIC WATERFALL REVENUE** without adopting a framework. **`ACR-1`: no standard is prescribed**** |
| **3** | ***"`PVR-22` cannot be answered until people are employed"*** | **Personal exposure of an individual acting as a promoter, trustee, de facto director or occupier of premises does not wait for an employment contract. Some of it attaches before any entity exists** | **Part VIII's 27 dimensions include exposures that are personal and pre-incorporation. **Mandate §8: never say the four *"are personally liable"* or *"are protected"* — the dimension is stated, not concluded**** |
| **4** | ***"Nothing can be done until the blueprint is found"*** | **The blueprint's absence is itself a settled finding, and **`FD-1`** classifies it `HISTORICAL / REFERENCE ONLY`. Its recovery would not change any of the 35 questions** | **`EV-B-01` `E0`/`E1`/`P0`; **`RS-9`**; absence corroborated at **`E2`** on five grounds; round five **`PV-1 … PV-6`** — still absent. **No percentage, budget or ratio here is inherited from it**** |
| **5** | ***"The waterfall cannot be discussed until percentages are set"*** | **The source says the opposite: ***"THE PRIORITY ORDER IS THE CONTROL; PERCENTAGES ARE A PLANNING TOOL."*** The order is fixed and is reproduced in Part XI; the percentages are the thing that is missing** | **[appendix.txt:6296–6300]** for the principle; **6301–6321** for the eight priorities; **`SR-22`** *`EXPLICITLY UNRESOLVED — illustrative only`*, ***"never publish as policy"*** **[S-F:1899]**** |
| **6** | ***"The four unpaid control roles cannot be analysed until they are appointed"*** | **They are named in the source and they are acting. What is missing is authority, not identity. Unpaid is not the same as unexposed** | **Part XVII's 14 dimensions; mandate §19: *"unpaid ≠ no liability"*. **The CEA's finding that the Internal Auditor row is `NONE IN PRACTICE` makes the absence of appointment the subject of the analysis, not a bar to it**** |
| **7** | ***"No question can be asked of counsel until the founder decides the entity"*** | **This is the most dangerous of the seven, and it is exactly backwards. Mandate §10 forbids this brief from selecting the entity; **`U-PD-15`** requires that counsel be asked a question that is not *"which option is correct"*; and **`DA-31`** forbids a successor instrument** | **[S-K(E): §XXVI]**: counsel must be given **7 items** and asked **`U-PD-15`'s question**, and the organisation may not pay because **`A-N16 REMAINS BLOCKED`** and **`PP-04`**. **Part XXIII is written to that instruction and to nothing else** |

## XXI.8 The one dependency, and the one act that removes it

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — **[S-F:1878]**; **[S-K(E): §XXVI]**; **U-PD-16**.

| Item | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **The dependency on which everything else waits** | **`SR-01` / `ID-1` / `LDR-U08`. **Sixteen of eighteen dependency steps sit behind it. Nineteen of twenty-two `PVR` items depend on it. It gates **`P0-A`** and **`P0-B`**. It is **`BLOCKER A / Tier 0`**, ranked first in the corpus's own dependency tree **[S-F:604]**** |
| **What its evidence field says** | ***"A written decision plus counsel opinion"*** — **[S-F:1878]** |
| **What its next-action field says** | ***"Commission counsel"*** — **[S-F:1878]** |
| **How many times that next action has been performed** | **Six times by six instruments, and a seventh time by this brief. **The register never advanced** — **U-PD-16** **[S-K(E): §XXVI]** |
| **Why six performances produced no advance** | **Because an instrument cannot commission. A document can describe a commission, specify its scope, list its deliverables and identify its performer — and it can do all of that correctly — and still not be the commission. **Only a person can send it. Only a person can sign it. Only a person can pay for it, and **`C-15`** and **`PP-04`** mean that person will have to pay personally, on a recorded founder decision, with qualified tax advice** |
| **The act that removes it** | **One natural person — **`U-PD-9`** — sends the instruction at **Part XXIII** to a qualified Indian professional, with the disclosure pack at **Part XXIV** attached, requiring the output format at **Part XXV**, and pays for it personally on a recorded founder decision** |
| **What this brief can do about it** | **Make the instruction copy-and-sendable, make the disclosure pack complete as a checklist without filling in a single field, and state the format in which the answer must come back. **That is Parts XXIII, XXIV and XXV, and it is all of it. **This brief is not the act**** |

> ### **`EVERY DEPENDENCY IN THIS PART RESOLVES, IN THE END, TO ONE FACT THAT IS NOT ARCHITECTURAL: WHETHER A HUMAN BEING HAS SENT A LETTER. THE ARCHITECTURE CANNOT SEND IT. NO PART OF THIS DOCUMENT CAN SEND IT. PART XXIII EXISTS SO THAT THE SENDING IS THE ONLY THING LEFT TO DO.`**

# PART XXII — BLOCKERS AND STOP CONDITIONS

## XXII.1 Four things that are routinely confused, and are not the same

**[P]** `[SOURCE]`. **Conflating these four is how an architecture acquires a false sense of progress: a prohibition is read as a task, a gate is read as a blocker, and a stop condition is read as a delay.**

| Term | **What it is** | **What it does** | **How it ends** | **Corpus anchor** |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **`BLOCKER`** | **A capability that may not activate until something is resolved** | **It withholds activation. **The corpus's own taxonomy classifies nine of the absent mechanisms as blockers — ***"the capability may not activate until resolved"*** **[S-F:158]**** | **By the thing being resolved — a decision, a validation, a build or a funding** | **[S-F] Part V, `BLK-A … BLK-F`** |
| **`PROHIBITION`** | **An act that may not be performed at all, in force now, irrespective of progress** | **It forbids conduct. **It is not a queue; it is a boundary** | **Only when its own stated condition is met — and for several, never by this organisation's own decision** | **`C-10 … C-29` **[S-K(E):1594–1613]**; `PP-01 … PP-09` **[S-J:1672–1684]**** |
| **`GATE`** | **A formal, conjunctive authorisation test with an evidence requirement** | **It decides whether something may be declared ready. ***"A PASS requires evidence, not assertion"*** — Rule F-14 **[S-F:968]**** | **By evidence being produced and recorded, sub-gate by sub-gate** | **`P0-A … P0-J` **[S-F:972–981]**** |
| **`STOP CONDITION`** | **A rule that requires work to cease and escalate when a specified situation arises** | **It halts activity already under way. **A blocker prevents starting; a stop condition ends what has started** | **It does not end. It is obeyed, then the matter is escalated** | ***"Stop-work and escalate, never enforce"*** — **`VG-1`**; **[S-D: §36.4 `VG-2`]** — any instruction to detain, search, confiscate, interrogate or expel is outside every role's authority; **§9.2 rules 1 and 2 **[S-A:719–726]**** |

> ### **`THIS BRIEF CONTAINS NO BLOCKER IT CAN REMOVE, NO PROHIBITION IT CAN LIFT, NO GATE IT CAN PASS AND NO STOP CONDITION IT CAN WAIVE. WHAT IT CONTAINS IS A PRECISE STATEMENT OF EACH, SO THAT NOTHING IS DONE IN IGNORANCE OF THEM.`**

## XXII.2 The corpus's blocker taxonomy — six categories, 83 items, counted

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — **[S-F] Part V, §§V.1–V.7.** Counts were produced by extracting every `BLK-` identifier in the instrument and de-duplicating. **No figure is estimated.**

| Category | **Section** | **Title, verbatim** | **Items** | **What the category means for this brief** |
|---|---|---|---|---|

## XXII.4 `GATE P0` — the pilot-authorisation gate, its conjunctive rule, and the error it exists to prevent

**[P]** `[SOURCE]` — **[S-F] §VIII.1, 964–981; Rule F-14 at 968; §VIII.2 at 983; §VIII.3 at 1001–1017; Rule F-15 at 1019.**

| Item | **Content** |
|---|---|
| **The gate** | **`P0-A` governance readiness · `P0-B` legal and compliance readiness · `P0-C` economic readiness · `P0-D` human-system readiness · `P0-E` leadership readiness · `P0-F` safety readiness · `P0-G` communication readiness · `P0-H` data and technology readiness · `P0-I` measurement readiness · `P0-J` funding and resource readiness **[S-F:972–981]**** |
| **The rule** | **Rule F-14, *"`P0` is conjunctive and recorded"*: *"All ten sub-gates must PASS. **A PASS requires evidence, not assertion.** The authorisation record names: the sub-gate, the evidence produced, the responsible authority, the date, and — where a sub-gate FAILs — whether the pilot is blocked or whether the blocked capability is withdrawn from scope"*** **[S-F:968]**** |
| **The escape the rule provides** | ***"The last option is the one that makes the gate usable: a FAIL on `P0-F` need not block a pilot that does no protection activity at all, provided the withdrawal is recorded and the capability is genuinely absent rather than nominally absent"*** **[S-F:968]**. **This brief records no withdrawal, because it authorises nothing** |
| **Current position** | ### **`GATE P0 = 0 OF 10 PASS`** — **[S-K(E):1609]**, in the `C-25` row; **CEA `GATE P0 = 0 of 10`**. **22 of 22 Tier-0 items outstanding** |
| **`P0-C` specifically** | ***"A bank account exists with ≥2 signatories and a continuity rule"*** **[S-F:974]** — this is `A-N16` expressed as a gate condition, and it is the reason **Part X** exists |
| **What P0 does not require** | ***"Any software, platform, app, dashboard or AI feature"* — **[S-A: §23.1]**, **[S-B: §40]** no technology; and *"a mark, palette, logo or visual identity"* — `N-E-02` blocked by `A-N12`, with **[S-E: Rule E-65]** permitting word-only identification **[S-F:985, 989–990]** |
| **What the gate exists to prevent** | ***"The organisation must not be declared pilot-ready merely because documents are complete — that is Rule F-1 and Part XVIII in operational form"*** **[S-F:966]**. **The error chain itself is reproduced below, verbatim** |

**The error `P0` exists to prevent, reproduced verbatim from **[S-F:1005–1016]** — because this brief is a document, and the error is a document-shaped error:**

```
DOCUMENTS COMPLETE
   -> "the architecture is comprehensive"
   -> "therefore we are ready"
   -> PILOT AUTHORISED
   -> first payment cannot lawfully be made       (LDR-U08)
   -> first public statement has no approved policy ([S: Ch.31])
   -> first incident has no insurance answer      (A-N10)
   -> first child activity has no certified safeguarding (A-B6)
   -> first data collection has no retention rule  (A-N26)
   -> PILOT HALTS, and the halt is attributed to "implementation problems"
      rather than to nine decisions that were never made
```

> ### **`RULE F-15: "A HALTED PILOT IS A GOVERNANCE FAILURE, NOT AN OPERATIONAL ONE." WHERE A PILOT HALTS BECAUSE A `P0` SUB-GATE WAS NOT SATISFIED, THE FINDING IS RECORDED AGAINST THE AUTHORISATION, NOT AGAINST THE OPERATION. **[S-F:1019]**`**

> ### **`THIS BRIEF IS THE SEVENTH DOCUMENT IN THIS SERIES. IT MUST NOT BE READ AS THE NEXT ARROW IN THAT CHAIN. IT DOES NOT MAKE THE ARCHITECTURE MORE COMPLETE; IT MAKES ONE MISSING ANSWER COMMISSIONABLE. A DOCUMENT THAT IS READ AS PROGRESS IS THE EXACT ERROR `GATE P0` WAS BUILT TO PREVENT.`**

| **Why that is not nothing** | **`U-PD-16` records that six instruments performed the next action and the register never advanced. **The reason is not analytical: no instrument can send a letter. **This brief is the seventh instrument, and it will not advance the register either, unless Part XXIII is sent by a person. **§XXX.2 makes that the verdict's operative qualification, and the last sentence of this document names the single act**** |
| **What this brief does not change** | **No blocker is removed. **No prohibition is lifted. **No gate is passed. **No stop condition is waived. **No decision is made. **No entity is selected. **No number is written. **No certainty is created** |
